## Canada Oil and Gas Diving Regulations {#canada-oil-and-gas-diving-regulations .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/88-600
:::

[CANADA OIL AND GAS OPERATIONS ACT](/eng/acts/O-7)

Registration 1988-12-07

Regulations Respecting the Safety of Diving Operations Conducted in
Connection with the Exploration or Drilling for or the Production,
Conservation, Processing or Transportation of Oil or Gas

P.C. 1988-2609 1988-12-07
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Whereas a copy of the proposed [Regulations respecting the safety of
diving operations conducted in connection with the exploration or
drilling for or the production, conservation, processing or
transportation of oil or gas](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-600),
substantially in the form annexed hereto, was published in the [Canada
Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) Part I on July 9, 1988 and a
reasonable opportunity was thereby afforded to interested persons to
make representations to the Minister of Energy, Mines and Resources or
the Minister of Indian Affairs and Northern Development with respect
thereto;

Therefore, Her Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the
recommendation of the Minister of Energy, Mines and Resources and the
Minister of Indian Affairs and Northern Development, pursuant to
sections 12[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#footnote_e){#footnote_e-ID0EFBAA} and 57 of the
Oil and Gas Production and Conservation Act, is pleased hereby to make
the annexed Regulations respecting the safety of diving operations
conducted in connection with the exploration or drilling for or the
production, conservation, processing or transportation of oil and gas.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#footnote_e-ID0EFBAA){#footnote_e}S.C. 1986, c.
  45, s. 122
  :::
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-916543 .Part}

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations may be
cited as the [Canada Oil and Gas Diving
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-600).

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-916545 .Part}

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** In these Regulations,

[[acceptable standard]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[acceptable standard]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an applicable
    standard that is acceptable to the Minister; ([norme
    acceptable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[accident]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[accident]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fortuitous event that
    results in the death of or injury to any person involved in a diving
    operation; ([accident]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Oil and Gas Production and
    Conservation Act; ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ADS]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ADS]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an atmospheric diving system
    capable of withstanding external pressures greater than atmospheric
    pressure and in which the internal pressure remains at atmospheric
    pressure and includes a one-person submarine and the one-atmosphere
    compartment of a diving submersible; ([système ADS]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[ADS dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ADS dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dive in which an ADS is
    used; ([plongée avec système ADS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ADS diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ADS diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving
    operation in which an ADS dive is made; ([opérations de plongée avec
    système ADS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ADS supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ADS supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a supervisor of a
    diving operation involving a pilot; ([(directeur de plongée avec
    système ADS)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ambient pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ambient pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the pressure at any
    given depth; ([pression ambiante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[appropriate breathing mixture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[appropriate breathing mixture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in
    relation to a diving operation, a breathing mixture that is
    suitable, in terms of composition, temperature and pressure, for the
    diving plant and equipment used in the diving operation, for the
    work to be undertaken and for the conditions under which and the
    depth at which the diving operation is to be conducted; ([mélange
    respiratoire approprié]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[attendant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[attendant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who has been
    trained in diving procedures and who is acting under the direction
    of a supervisor; ([adjoint]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[bell bounce diving technique]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[bell bounce diving technique]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving
    procedure whereby a diving bell or diving submersible is used to
    transport divers who are under atmospheric pressure or pressures
    greater than atmospheric pressure to a work site and subsequently to
    transport the divers under pressures greater than atmospheric
    pressure from an underwater work site, but does not include
    saturation diving techniques; ([technique de la plongée
    d'incursion]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[bottom time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[bottom time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period commencing when
    a person begins pressurization or descent for a dive and terminating
    when the person commences decompression or ascent; ([durée du séjour
    au fond]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[breathing mixture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[breathing mixture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a mixture of gases
    used for human respiration and includes pure oxygen and any
    therapeutic mixture; ([mélange respiratoire]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[category I dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[category I dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dive to a depth of
    less than 50 m using surface-oriented diving techniques and a
    breathing mixture of air, but no other breathing mixture except in
    cases of decompression, treatment or emergency, and includes a dive
    in which a diving bell or diving submersible is used for an
    observation dive, but does not include a lock-out dive; ([plongée de
    catégorie I]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[category I diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[category I diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving
    operation in which a category I dive is made; ([opérations de
    plongée de catégorie I]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[category II dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[category II dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dive in which a
    diving bell or diving submersible is used for a lock-out dive to a
    depth of less than 50 m using a breathing mixture of air, or to a
    depth of 50 m or more using a breathing mixture of mixed gas other
    than air, but does not include a saturation dive; ([plongée de
    catégorie II]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[category II diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[category II diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving
    operation in which a category II dive is made; ([opérations de
    plongée de catégorie II]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[category III dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[category III dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a saturation dive
    and any dive other than an ADS dive, a category I dive or a category
    II dive; ([plongée de catégorie III]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[category III diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[category III diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving
    operation in which a category III dive is made; ([opérations de
    plongée de catégorie III]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[compression chamber]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[compression chamber]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a pressure vessel
    that is suitable for human occupancy at internal pressures greater
    than atmospheric pressure; ([caisson de compression ou compartiment
    de compression]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[contingency plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[contingency plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a contingency plan
    referred to in paragraph 4(4)(i); ([plan d'urgence]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any vessel, vehicle, hovercraft,
    semi-submersible, submarine or diving-submersible and includes a
    self-propelled, tethered, towed or bottom-contact apparatus, but
    does not include an installation; ([véhicule]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[decompression]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[decompression]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the gradual reduction of
    the pressures of the inert components of a breathing mixture in the
    body; ([décompression]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[decompression sickness]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[decompression sickness]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a condition
    caused by the reduction or other changes of pressure on or in the
    body; ([maladie de la décompression]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[decompression sickness type I]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[decompression sickness type I]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    decompression sickness that is characterized by one or both of the
    following symptoms, namely,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pain that is located at or near the joints of the
      limbs but is not felt in other parts of the body, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cutaneous manifestations including a rash and
      cutaneous pruritis (intense itching); ([maladie de la
      décompression de type I]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[decompression sickness type II]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[decompression sickness type II]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    decompression sickness that is characterized by one or more of the
    following symptoms, namely,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} neurological manifestations related to the
      central nervous system,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} interference with the respiratory or
      cardiovascular system,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} otologic disorders, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any symptoms not referred to in the definition
      "decompression sickness type I"; ([maladie de la décompression de
      type II]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[decompression table]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[decompression table]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a table or set of
    tables that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shows a schedule of rates for safe descent and
      ascent and the appropriate breathing mixture to be used by a diver
      during a dive, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been approved in accordance with section 5;
      ([table de décompression]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dive site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dive site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the place on a craft or
    installation from which a diving operation is conducted and from
    which a diver or pilot involved in the diving operation enters the
    water; ([lieu de plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dive time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dive time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period commencing when a
    person begins pressurization or descent for a dive and terminating
    when the person completes decompression or ascent; ([durée de la
    plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who meets the
    requirements of section 53, 55 or 57, who is involved in a diving
    operation that is part of a diving program and who may be subject to
    pressures greater than atmospheric pressure;
    ([plongeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diving bell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving bell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a compression chamber that
    is intended to be submerged and that is designed to transport a
    person at atmospheric pressure or divers at pressures greater than
    atmospheric pressure from the surface to an underwater work site and
    back and includes the compression chamber of a diving submersible;
    ([tourelle de plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diving contractor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving contractor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who employs
    a diver for a diving operation or who holds a contract to supply
    diving services for a diving operation, but does not include a
    self-employed diver; ([entrepreneur en plongée]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[diving crew]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving crew]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the persons who are
    designated by a diving contractor to be involved in a diving
    operation conducted by the diving contractor and who are under the
    supervision of a supervisor; ([équipe de plongée]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[diving doctor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving doctor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a medical doctor who is
    licensed and registered to practise in a province, who has completed
    a diving medical course acceptable to the Minister and who has been
    accepted in writing by the Minister to certify divers for the
    purposes of paragraph 53(b), but who has not been accepted by the
    Minister to provide medical assistance under pressures greater than
    atmospheric pressure; ([médecin de plongée]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any work or activity
    that is associated with a dive and that takes place during the total
    dive time and includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any work or activity involving a diver or pilot,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the activities of a person assisting a diver or
      pilot involved in the dive, and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any use of an ADS in the dive; ([opérations de
      plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diving plant and equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving plant and equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the plant
    and equipment that are used in, or in connection with, a diving
    operation and includes the plant and equipment that are essential to
    a diver or pilot; ([matériel de plongée]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[diving program]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving program]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any work or activity
    related to the exploration or drilling for or the production,
    conservation, processing or transportation of oil or gas that
    involves a diving operation; ([programme de plongée ou
    programme]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diving safety specialist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving safety specialist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who
    meets the criteria set out in subsection 26(1); ([spécialiste de la
    sécurité en plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diving station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the place from which a
    diving operation is controlled; ([poste de commande de
    plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[diving submersible]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving submersible]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a self-propelled
    submarine that has at least

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one one-atmosphere compartment from which the
      diving submersible is piloted and from which a dive can be
      supervised, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one compression chamber from which a dive can be
      conducted; ([sous-marin crache-plongeurs]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[diving supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[diving supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a supervisor of a
    diving operation involving a diver; ([directeur de
    plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dressed-in]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dressed-in]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means fully equipped to dive and
    ready to enter the water, with the diver's personal diving equipment
    tested and at hand, whether or not helmet, face plate or face mask
    is in place; ([équipé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an exceptional situation
    resulting from an accident or incident; ([urgence]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[environmental conditions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[environmental conditions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means conditions
    that may affect a diving operation and includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} weather and sea conditions,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} speed of currents and tides,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} shipping activities,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} air and water temperatures,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} icing conditions, and

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} debris on the sea surface or sea bed;
      ([conditions ambiantes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hyperbaric first-aid technician]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hyperbaric first-aid technician]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    person who has successfully completed an advanced hyperbaric
    first-aid course acceptable to the Minister; ([secouriste
    hyperbare]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[incident]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[incident]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fortuitous event that
    compromises or is likely to compromise the safety of, or endangers
    or is likely to endanger the health, well-being or life of, a person
    involved in a diving operation; ([incident]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[installation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[installation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any fixed offshore
    structure used in connection with the exploration or drilling for or
    the production, conservation, processing or transportation of oil or
    gas; ([installation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[lifeline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[lifeline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a safety line attached to a
    diver that is suitable for recovering and lifting the diver and the
    diver's personal diving equipment from the water; ([ligne de
    vie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[life-support system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[life-support system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a system
    comprising the breathing mixture supply systems, decompression and
    recompression equipment, environmental control systems and equipment
    and supplies that may be required to provide safe accommodation for
    a person in the water, in a compression chamber, in a diving bell,
    in a diving submersible or in an ADS under all pressures and
    conditions that a person may be exposed to during a diving
    operation; ([système de survie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[life-support technician]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[life-support technician]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who
    has successfully completed a life-support technician's course
    acceptable to the Minister and who has satisfied the Minister that
    the person has attained a level of competence in all aspects of all
    types of diving techniques, including emergency procedures,
    hyperbaric first-aid and operation of life-support systems;
    ([technicien des systèmes de survie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[lock-out dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[lock-out dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dive from a diving
    bell or a diving submersible; ([plongée à partir d'un
    sas]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[maximum working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[maximum working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the total weight
    of a load likely to be handled under normal operating conditions in
    a diving operation, weighed out of water and includes the weight of
    the umbilical; ([charge de service maximale]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[maximum working pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[maximum working pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the maximum
    pressure to which a compression chamber can safely be exposed under
    normal operating conditions in a diving operation, and, where a
    compression chamber is interconnected with one or more other
    compression chambers, means, in respect of each of those
    interconnected chambers, the maximum pressure to which the
    interconnected chamber with the lowest maximum working pressure can
    safely be exposed under normal operating conditions; ([pression de
    service maximale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[medical lock]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[medical lock]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a lock through which
    objects may be passed into or out of a compression chamber while a
    person inside the compression chamber remains under pressure; ([sas
    à médicaments]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in relation to any area in respect of which the
      Minister of Indian Affairs and Northern Development has
      administrative responsibility for the natural resources therein,
      the Minister of Indian Affairs and Northern Development, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in relation to any area in respect of which the
      Minister of Energy, Mines and Resources has administrative
      responsibility for the natural resources therein, the Minister of
      Energy, Mines and Resources; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who has been
    authorized, pursuant to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act, to carry on
    a work or activity referred to in section 3.1 of the Act that is a
    diving program or that includes a diving program;
    ([exploitant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[personal diving equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[personal diving equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the diving
    equipment carried by a diver on the diver's person during a dive and
    includes a diving suit, breathing apparatus, bale-out gas bottle and
    communications equipment; ([équipement personnel de
    plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pilot]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pilot]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who controls the
    movement of an ADS from within the ADS and who performs from within
    the ADS any other tasks necessary for the operation of the ADS;
    ([pilote]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pressure vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pressure vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a closed container
    capable of withstanding internal or external pressures, or both,
    greater than one atmosphere; ([appareil sous
    pression]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[procedures manual]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[procedures manual]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the procedures
    manual referred to in paragraph 4(4)(a); ([manuel des
    méthodes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[recognized body]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[recognized body]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an organization, a
    classification society, a certifying agency, a group of persons or
    an individual that is acceptable to the Minister as having the
    expertise and experience to set standards for, or to inspect and
    certify, diving plant and equipment or parts thereof; ([autorité
    reconnue]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[saturation dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[saturation dive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dive in which
    saturation diving techniques are used; ([plongée à
    saturation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[saturation diving technique]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[saturation diving technique]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving
    procedure that essentially equilibrates the total pressure of inert
    gases in the body of a diver with the ambient pressure and allows
    extended periods of bottom time without additional decompression
    time required; ([technique de la plongée à
    saturation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[SCUBA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[SCUBA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a self-contained open-circuit
    underwater breathing apparatus; ([appareil de plongée
    autonome]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[skip]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[skip]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a stage, cage, basket or wet bell
    in which a diver may be lowered to or raised from an underwater work
    site; ([skip]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[specialized diving doctor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[specialized diving doctor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diving
    doctor who has completed an advanced diving medical course
    acceptable to the Minister and who has been accepted in writing by
    the Minister to provide medical assistance under pressures greater
    than atmospheric pressure; ([médecin de plongée
    spécialisé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[stand-by diver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[stand-by diver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a diver who is
    dressed-in and trained to operate at the same depths and in the same
    circumstances as the diver for whom the stand-by diver is standing
    by, who is at the same dive site as the other diver and who is
    available without delay to assist that other diver; ([plongeur de
    secours]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person appointed in
    writing by a diving contractor, pursuant to subsection 9(3), as a
    diving supervisor or an ADS supervisor to supervise a diving
    operation; ([directeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[surface compression chamber]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[surface compression chamber]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    compression chamber that is not intended to be submerged; ([caisson
    de compression de surface]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[surface-oriented diving technique]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[surface-oriented diving technique]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    diving procedure in which the use of a diving bell or diving
    submersible is not required; ([technique de la plongée avec soutien
    en surface]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[total dive time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[total dive time]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period commencing
    when a person begins to prepare for a dive and terminating when the
    person leaves the water, is not subject to pressures greater than
    atmospheric pressure and, in accordance with the relevant schedule
    in the appropriate decompression table, has normal inert gas
    pressure in the person's body; ([durée totale de la
    plongée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[umbilical]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[umbilical]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a composite hose or cable or
    number of separate hoses or cables capable of supplying a breathing
    mixture, power, heat, communications and other services, as required
    for a diving operation. ([ombilical]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

## [Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-916640 .Part}

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations apply to
any diving operation carried on in connection with the exploration or
drilling for or the production, conservation, processing or
transportation of oil or gas in areas to which the Act applies.

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Proposed Diving Programs]{.HTitleText1} {#h-916642 .Part}

### [Authorization]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916643 .Subheading}

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person
  may apply for an authorization under paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act in
  respect of a proposed diving program by forwarding to the Minister an
  application, completed in triplicate, in the form prescribed by the
  Minister.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An authorization under paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act
  in respect of a proposed diving program is, in addition to any other
  requirements of these Regulations, subject to the requirements that
  the operator and the diving contractor, if any, of the diving program
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} maintain the level of performance of the diving
    crew, diving plant and equipment and any craft or installation used
    in the diving program at or above the level of performance indicated
    in the application referred to in subsection (1) and accepted by the
    Minister, for the authorization for the diving program, as the level
    of performance at which the diving program will be carried on;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the operator or the diving contractor, as the
    case may be, proposes to replace a supervisor involved in the diving
    program or appoint an additional supervisor, provide the Minister
    with evidence that any replacement or additional supervisor who will
    be involved in the diving program meets the criteria set out in
    section 27, 29, 31 or 33 to supervise the category of dive the
    supervisor will be supervising; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where, in any area in which the diving program is
    being carried on, the environmental conditions, during any period,
    become more severe than the environmental conditions indicated in
    the application as being the most severe environmental conditions
    under which the diving program would be carried on, cease to carry
    on the diving program in that area during that period.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No authorization may be given under paragraph
  3.2(1)(b) of the Act in respect of a proposed diving program unless
  the applicant provides the Minister with evidence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that a diving safety specialist was consulted on
    all safety aspects of the diving program;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that a diving safety specialist will be available
    on a 24 hour a day basis to advise any person involved in the diving
    program, including any person making decisions affecting the safety
    of divers involved in the diving program, on all safety aspects of
    the diving program;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that any supervisor who will be involved in the
    diving program meets the criteria set out in section 27, 29, 31 or
    33 to supervise the category of dive the supervisor will be
    supervising;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} that the services of a specialized diving doctor,
    who is familiar with the diving procedures to be used in the diving
    operation that will form part of the diving program and who is
    within a travelling distance of the diving operation that is
    acceptable to the Minister, will be available on a 24 hour a day
    basis to any person involved in the diving program;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} of any certificates issued by the manufacturer or a
    recognized body in respect of the diving plant and equipment to be
    used in the diving program; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} where a diving program is to be conducted by a
    diving contractor who is not also the operator of the diving
    program, that the diving contractor is able to meet any liability
    for loss, damage, costs or expenses that may be incurred by the
    diving contractor as a result of the diving program.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} No authorization may be given under paragraph
  3.2(1)(b) of the Act in respect of a proposed diving program unless
  approval has been granted by the Minister for the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the procedures manual that contains the procedures
    to be followed in the diving program, including the procedures
    referred to in Schedule I;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the diving plant and equipment to be used in the
    diving program, including the two-compartment compression chamber
    referred to in subparagraph 9(5)(h)(ii) and paragraph 47(3)(a), and
    any equivalent first-aid supplies referred to in paragraph 12(2)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} schematic drawings showing the general arrangement
    of any diving plant and equipment to be used in the diving program
    and their location on board the craft or installation on which or
    from which they will be used in the diving program;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where a craft is to be used in the diving program
    and is to be used in a manner referred to in subparagraph
    12(2)(p)(iv), to be approved in accordance with section 5, the
    manner in which the craft is to be used;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any use in the diving program of a craft in the
    dynamically positioned mode and the dynamically positioned diving
    operational capacity graph in respect of the craft;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} where a diving submersible is to be used in the
    diving program and is to be secured in a manner referred to in
    subparagraph 18(b)(iii), to be approved in accordance with section
    5, the manner in which the diving submersible is to be secured;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any experimental equipment or technique to be used
    in the program;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any evacuation, rescue and treatment facilities and
    devices provided for the program pursuant to section 23; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the contingency plan to be followed in the diving
    program including the emergency procedures referred to in Schedule
    II and the particulars of any additional evacuation, rescue and
    treatment facilities and devices to be provided for use in the
    diving program.

<!-- -->

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister is authorized to grant, in accordance with subsection (2),
  any approval prescribed in these Regulations and to make that approval
  subject to, in addition to the requirements prescribed in these
  Regulations, such requirements, terms or conditions as the Minister
  determines.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall provide a person with evidence of
  any approval granted to the person pursuant to subsection (1).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister is authorized to suspend or revoke an
  approval referred to in subsection (1) for failure to comply with, or
  for contravention of, the terms and conditions subject to which the
  approval was granted.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister, pursuant to subsection (3),
  suspends or revokes an approval granted to a person, the Minister
  shall give the person an opportunity to show cause why the approval
  should not be suspended or revoked.

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Operators]{.HTitleText1} {#h-916672 .Part}

### [Duties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916673 .Subheading}

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of a diving program shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} arrange for the services of a diving safety
    specialist who will be available on a 24 hour a day basis to advise
    any person involved in the diving program, including any person
    making decisions affecting the safety of divers involved in the
    diving program, on all safety aspects of the diving program;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make available a suitable place from which any
    diving operation that is part of the diving program may be
    conducted;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to the extent practicable, give advance notice of
    any operation that is part of the diving program to the person in
    charge of any craft or installation in the vicinity of the
    operation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} make available adequate forecasts of environmental
    conditions to the supervisor on duty at a diving operation that is
    part of the diving program

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} before the diving operation begins, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} during the diving operation, at intervals of not
      more than 24 hours and at any time where the supervisor requests
      those forecasts;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} inform the supervisor on duty at a diving operation
    that is part of the diving program of any matter within the
    operator's control that may affect the safety of the diving
    operation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} provide an adequate and effective system of
    communication between the supervisor who is on duty and any person,
    other than the divers and pilots, involved in, or in a position to
    assist in, a diving operation that is part of the diving program,
    including a winch or crane operator and a person on the bridge, on
    the rig floor or in the main control room of a craft or installation
    used in the diving operation;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} while a diving operation that is part of the diving
    program is in progress, prominently display notices to that effect

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any craft or installation used in
      the diving operation, on the bridge and in the engine room, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any diving plant and equipment
      used in the diving operation, on any controls the operation of
      which might endanger a diver or pilot and on any controls for
      impressed current cathodic protection;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} display in the control room of a craft that will be
    operated in the dynamically positioned mode in a diving operation
    that is part of the diving program a copy of the dynamically
    positioned diving operational capacity graph in respect of the
    craft;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the event that a member of a diving crew
    involved in the diving program meets with an accident, notify the
    Minister of the accident by the most rapid and practicable means and
    submit to the Minister a report of the accident in the form set out
    in Schedule III;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} in the event of a serious illness affecting a
    member of a diving crew involved in the diving program or an
    incident in connection with the diving program, notify the Minister
    of the illness or incident as soon as possible, investigate the
    cause of the illness or incident and submit to the Minister a report
    of that illness or incident, including, in the case of an incident,
    a report in the form set out in Schedule III;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} submit to the Minister a monthly report of all
    injuries to any member of a diving crew involved in a diving
    operation that is part of the diving program; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} during the course of any diving operation that is
    part of the diving program, display a copy of the authorization
    under paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act for that diving program and
    evidence of any approval granted in relation to that authorization
    pursuant to section 5 in a prominent place at the diving station for
    the diving operation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator of a diving program shall not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct any diving operation that is part of the
    diving program in the vicinity of any other activity that might pose
    a danger to any person involved in the diving operation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use, in a diving operation, any craft that has
    insufficient power or stability for the safe conduct of the diving
    operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prevent any diving contractor involved in the
    diving program from complying with any of the provisions of these
    Regulations.

### [Changes in Equipment and Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916696 .Subheading}

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsection (2), the operator of a diving program shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} forthwith repair, replace or alter or arrange for
    the repair, replacement or alteration of any diving plant and
    equipment that is being used in the diving program and that is
    defective or becomes inadequate or unsafe;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} alter any procedure set out in the procedures
    manual for the diving program that becomes unsafe, inadequate or
    deficient; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where necessary, initiate a new procedure in
    respect of the diving program.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to subsection (1), the operator of a
  diving program

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} repairs, replaces or alters or arranges for the
    repair, replacement or alteration of any diving plant and equipment
    referred to in paragraph (1)(a), other than a routine repair,
    replacement or alteration carried out by a competent person,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} alters a procedure set out in the procedures manual
    for the diving program, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} initiates a new procedure in respect of the diving
    program,

  the operator shall, prior to any activity referred to in
  paragraphs (a) to (c), obtain such approval as the Minister determines
  pursuant to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act.

### [Authorization]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916707 .Subheading}

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator may apply for authorization pursuant to subsection 12.2(1) of
  the Act to use equipment, methods, measures or standards that do not
  comply with these Regulations.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application referred to in subsection (1) shall
  set out the manner in which the equipment, methods, measures or
  standards that are the subject of the application provide a level of
  safety and pollution prevention at least equivalent to that which
  would be provided by compliance with these Regulations.

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Diving Contractors]{.HTitleText1} {#h-916711 .Part}

### [Duties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916712 .Subheading}

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsection (2), no diving contractor shall conduct a diving
  operation unless the diving contractor has arranged for the services
  of a diving safety specialist, other than the diving safety specialist
  available pursuant to paragraph 4(3)(b), who will be available on a 24
  hour a day basis to advise any person involved in the diving
  operation, including any person making decisions affecting the safety
  of divers involved in the diving operation, on all safety aspects of
  the diving operation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the operator referred to in subsection 6(1) and
  the diving contractor referred to in subsection (1) are the same
  person, the diving safety specialist required pursuant to
  subsection (1) may be the same person as the diving safety specialist
  required pursuant to paragraph 4(3)(b).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No diving contractor shall conduct a diving operation
  that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a category I dive unless the diving contractor has
    appointed in writing a person who meets the criteria set out in
    section 27, 29 or 31 to supervise the diving operation and such a
    supervisor is present at all times during the diving operation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a category II dive unless the diving contractor has
    appointed in writing a person who meets the criteria set out in
    section 29 or 31 to supervise the diving operation and such a
    supervisor is present at all times during the diving operation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a category III dive unless the diving contractor
    has appointed in writing a person who meets the criteria set out in
    section 31 to supervise the diving operation and such a supervisor
    is present at all times during the diving operation; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the use of an ADS unless the diving contractor has
    appointed in writing a person who meets the criteria set out in
    section 33 to supervise the diving operation and such a supervisor
    is present at all times during the diving operation.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} No diving contractor shall, in a diving operation
  conducted by the diving contractor, employ a person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to make a category I dive unless the person meets
    the criteria set out in section 53, 55 or 57;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to make a category II dive unless the person meets
    the criteria set out in section 55 or 57;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to make a category III dive unless the person meets
    the criteria set out in section 57; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to pilot an ADS unless the person meets the
    criteria set out in section 64.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A diving contractor who conducts a diving operation
  that is part of a diving program shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that every diving supervisor employed by the
    diving contractor in the diving program on a full-time basis has the
    opportunity to supervise each year a minimum of 12 dives appropriate
    to the category of dive for which the supervisor is certified;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that every ADS supervisor employed by the
    diving contractor in the diving program on a full-time basis has the
    opportunity to supervise each year a minimum of six ADS dives;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that every diver employed by the diving
    contractor in the diving program on a full-time basis has the
    opportunity to make each year a minimum of 24 dives, totalling a
    minimum of 20 hours of bottom time, appropriate to the category of
    dive for which the diver is certified;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensure that every pilot employed by the diving
    contractor in the diving program on a full-time basis has the
    opportunity to make each year at least four ADS dives totalling a
    minimum of 16 hours of bottom time;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} ensure that, except in the case of an emergency,
    each member of a diving crew involved in the diving operation, in
    every 24 hour period,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has a rest period of not less than eight
      consecutive hours, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is required to work not more than 12 hours;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} follow the procedures set out in the procedures
    manual for the diving program and any altered or newly initiated
    procedures referred to in section 7 for the diving program;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} maintain, at the craft or installation from which
    the diving operation is conducted, two copies of these Regulations
    and a copy of the applicable procedures manual and make them
    available to any person involved or to be involved in the diving
    operation and, on request, to the Minister or a conservation
    engineer;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} provide or arrange for the provision of any diving
    plant and equipment necessary for the safe conduct of the diving
    operation, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} adequate firefighting equipment, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a two-compartment compression chamber that

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} has been approved for the diving program, in
        accordance with section 5, for use at a pressure that is not
        less than six atmospheres absolute and, where the maximum
        working pressure that may be encountered during any dive that is
        part of the diving operation is greater than six atmospheres
        absolute, for use at the maximum pressure plus one atmosphere,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} is suitable for the diving operation, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} is located in a readily accessible place on
        board the craft or installation from which the diving operation
        is conducted, except in the case of a diving operation that is
        conducted at a depth of 10 m or less, and, where the supervisor
        of the diving operation approves, may be located within one
        hour's travelling time from the dive site;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} use only diving plant and equipment that is of
    sound construction, adequate strength, free from patent defects and
    in good working order;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} provide for the protection of the diving plant and
    equipment used in the diving operation from malfunction in the
    environmental conditions under which the diving plant and equipment
    are to be used, including conditions of low or high temperatures;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} permit only such repair, replacement and alteration
    of diving plant and equipment used in the diving operation as has
    been approved pursuant to subsection 7(2) and ensure that routine
    repair, replacement or alteration is carried out by a competent
    person;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} provide adequate illumination of the dive site and
    the underwater work site of the diving operation

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} during any period of darkness or low visibility,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the supervisor of the diving operation
      requests the illumination and where the nature of the diving
      operation so permits;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} provide a diving operations logbook that is
    permanently bound and has numbered pages;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} retain any diving operations logbook referred to in
    paragraph (m) that is delivered to the diving contractor by a
    supervisor pursuant to subsection 50(5), and any records or copies
    delivered to the diving contractor by a supervisor pursuant to
    subsection 52(3), for a period of not less than two years after the
    day of the last entry made in it; and

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} produce, on request, any logbooks, records or
    copies referred to in paragraph (n) for inspection by the Minister
    or a conservation engineer.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where continuance of a diving operation would
  compromise or is likely to compromise the safety or would endanger or
  is likely to endanger the health, well-being or life of, any person
  involved in the diving operation, the diving contractor who conducts
  the diving operation shall forthwith interrupt or discontinue the
  diving operation.

<!-- -->

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  diving contractor who conducts a diving operation shall not permit any
  person involved in the diving operation to be exposed to a pressure
  greater than atmospheric pressure in a compression chamber used in
  connection with the diving operation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not more than 12 months prior to the day of the
    exposure, a diving doctor has certified that the person is fit to be
    exposed to the pressure; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} copies of the certificate referred to in
    paragraph (a) are in the possession of the diving contractor and the
    person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to any person who requires therapeutic compression;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an emergency, to a person who can
    provide medical treatment, where no person certified pursuant to
    paragraph (1)(a) is available.

### [Examination and Testing of Diving Plant and Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916759 .Subheading}

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  diving contractor who conducts a diving operation shall use or permit
  to be used in the diving operation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} only diving plant and equipment that has been
    examined and, where appropriate, subjected to a pressure leak test
    using an appropriate breathing mixture to a pressure that is not
    less than six atmospheres absolute and, where the maximum working
    pressure that may be encountered during any dive that is part of the
    diving operation is greater than six atmospheres absolute, to the
    maximum pressure plus one atmosphere

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} not more than three months prior to the day on
      which it is to be used,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on mobilization and assembly, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} following any repair, replacement or alteration
      of the diving plant and equipment that might affect its safety;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a compression chamber, only a
    compression chamber that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} not more than two years prior to the day on which
      it is to be used, has been subjected to a pressure leak test to
      the maximum working pressure of the chamber using an appropriate
      breathing mixture, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} not more than five years prior to the day on
      which it is to be used, has been subjected to an internal pressure
      test of at least 1.25 times the maximum working pressure of the
      chamber;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a pressure vessel for compressed
    gases that is not intended to be immersed in water, including
    compressed air cylinders, only a pressure vessel that has been
    subjected to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a thorough examination and internal pressure test
      not more than five years prior to the day on which it is to be
      used, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an internal inspection for corrosion not more
      than two years prior to the day on which it is to be used, or such
      longer period as is determined by the Minister pursuant to
      paragraph 3.2 (1)(b) of the Act;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a pressure vessel for compressed
    gases that is intended to be immersed in water, only a pressure
    vessel that has been subjected to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a thorough examination and internal pressure test
      not more than two years prior to the day on which it is to be
      used, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an internal inspection for corrosion not more
      than one year prior to the day on which it is to be used, or such
      longer period as is determined by the Minister pursuant to
      paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of lifting equipment for a launch and
    recovery system, only lifting equipment that has been tested

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on first installation and, thereafter, before
      operational use of the lifting equipment following a repair,
      replacement or alteration, other than a routine repair,
      replacement or alteration carried out by a competent person, by
      means of a functional test, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} every six months following a functional test
      carried out pursuant to subparagraph (i), by means of a test that
      tests the capability of the lifting equipment to operate safely
      under its maximum working load.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A diving contractor who conducts a diving operation
  shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} each examination and test required to be carried
    out for the purposes of subsection (1) is carried out by or under
    the supervision of a recognized body and in accordance with an
    acceptable standard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where a pneumatic or hydrostatic pressure test is
    carried out for the purposes of subsection (1), adequate precautions
    are taken to ensure the safety of the personnel involved, the diving
    plant and equipment and the craft or installation used in the test.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A diving contractor who conducts a diving operation
  shall keep a register in which are inserted or to which are attached
  certificates

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} containing details and results of examinations and
    tests carried out pursuant to subsection (1), and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} signed by the person by whom or under whose
    supervision the examinations or tests were carried out,

  and shall retain the register

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a register that contains
    certificates relating to pressure vessels, for at least five years
    after the day of the last entry in it; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, for at least two years after the
    day of the last entry in it.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A diving contractor who conducts a diving operation
  shall not use or permit to be used in the diving operation any diving
  plant and equipment that is unsafe as determined by an examination or
  test carried out pursuant to subsection (1).

### [Diving Plant and Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916788 .Subheading}

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  diving contractor who conducts a diving operation shall not use or
  permit to be used any diving plant and equipment in the diving
  operation unless the design thereof is such that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it enables divers and pilots to safely enter and
    leave the water;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} divers can be safely compressed or decompressed in
    accordance with the relevant schedule in the appropriate
    decompression table;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where a hot-water system is used as the means of
    heating a diver, a hot-water reservoir is, where practicable,
    included in the system; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the body temperature of a diver or pilot can be
    maintained within safe limits during the diving operation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A diving contractor who conducts a diving operation
  shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prior to the time a diver involved in the diving
    operation enters the water, the diver is provided with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a diving harness complete with pelvic support and
      lifting ring,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a depth indicator that is, where practicable, a
      type that can be monitored from the surface, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} during any period of darkness or low visibility
      or where requested by the supervisor of the diving operation, a
      lamp or other suitable device that indicates the diver's location;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the first-aid supplies listed in Part I of Schedule
    IV, or equivalent first-aid supplies approved in accordance with
    section 5 for the diving program of which the diving operation is a
    part, are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} packed in such a manner that they fit through the
      medical lock of any surface compression chamber used in the diving
      operation, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} kept on the craft or installation from which the
      diving operation is conducted, except where it is impracticable in
      a category I diving operation or an ADS diving operation and where
      the supervisor of the diving operation approves, in which case the
      supplies may be kept readily available within a travelling
      distance of the diving operation that is acceptable to the
      supervisor

    and, where a diving bell or diving submersible is used in the diving
    operation, the first-aid supplies listed in Part II of Schedule IV,
    or equivalent first-aid supplies approved in accordance with section
    5 for the diving program of which the diving operation is a part,
    are kept in the diving bell or the compression chamber of the diving
    submersible;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any airtight container used to pack any of the
    first-aid supplies referred to in paragraph (b) for use in the
    diving operation has a suitable means of equalizing pressure;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the safe use of the diving plant and
    equipment depends on the pressure or depth at which it is used, the
    diving plant and equipment is clearly marked with the maximum
    working pressure or the maximum depth at which it may be used;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any lifeline used in the diving operation has a
    manufacturer's breaking strength rating in accordance with an
    acceptable standard;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any gas bottle used in the diving operation is
    clearly marked with the name and chemical formula of its contents;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any winch used in the diving operation to raise or
    lower a skip, diving bell, diving submersible or ADS

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is so constructed that

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} a brake or mechanical locking device is applied
        when the control lever, handle or switch is not held in the
        operating position,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the brakes have the capability of stopping and
        holding 100 per cent of the maximum working load with the
        outermost layer of wire on the drum,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the brakes engage automatically on loss of
        power, and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} the lowering and raising of loads is controlled
        by power drives independent of the brake mechanism,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is not fitted with a pawl and ratchet gear on
      which the pawl has to be disengaged before commencing a lowering
      or raising operation,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is so designed as to prevent the possibility of
      freeze-up when in operation,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with a lifting wire capable of
      withstanding a functional test in accordance with an acceptable
      standard, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} complies with an acceptable standard of
      construction for winches;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any prime mover used in the diving operation to
    operate lifting equipment for a skip, diving bell, diving
    submersible or ADS is not used for any other purpose;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} except where alternative lifting equipment is
    provided for any skip, diving bell, diving submersible or ADS used
    in the diving operation, an auxiliary prime mover capable of lifting
    the maximum working load is provided;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} where, during the diving operation, a skip, diving
    bell, diving submersible or ADS is being held in position by a
    hydraulically operated winch that is not equipped with a mechanical
    locking device, the hydraulic pumps are kept running during the
    diving operation;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister so determines, pursuant to
    paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act, any craft or installation used in
    the diving operation is equipped with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a receiver system that is compatible with the
      relocation transponder system fitted to any diving bell, diving
      submersible or ADS that is used in the diving operation, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a hand-held receiver that is suitable for use by
      a diver or pilot in achieving a final location and that is
      compatible with the receiver system on the craft or installation
      and the relocation transponder system on the diving bell, diving
      submersible or ADS;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} any skip, diving submersible or ADS used in the
    diving operation is equipped with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a secondary lifting eye or similar device that is
      of at least the same strength as the primary lifting eye, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where practicable, an additional cable in the
      form of a suitable tag rope so designed that, in the event that
      the primary lifting cable breaks during a water-air interface
      transport, the tag rope will not permit the skip, diving
      submersible or ADS to descend to a depth greater than 25 m,

    and has readily available, for use in an emergency, a secondary
    lifting cable that has at least the same strength as the primary
    lifting cable and that is compatible with the secondary lifting eye
    or similar device;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} any skip used in the diving operation to transport
    divers through the water-air interface is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} large enough to carry, in uncramped conditions,
      at least two divers with their personal diving equipment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} secured against tipping or spinning,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} not encumbered by any equipment that may
      interfere with an occupant's foothold or handhold,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} equipped with handholds arranged in such a
      manner that crushed-hand injuries during launch or recovery are
      avoided,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} so constructed or equipped that its occupants are
      secure against falling out of the skip, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a skip that is a wet bell,
      equipped with an additional band mask or full face mask;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} any diving submersible or ADS used in the diving
    operation is equipped with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a stroboscopic light that is automatically
      activated in the water and a pinger that operates at a frequency
      of 37.5 kHz, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a relocation transponder system that operates at
      a frequency of 37.5 kHz, where the Minister so determines pursuant
      to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} a secondary source of power that will operate in
    the event of a failure of the primary source of power is provided
    for the diving operation, can be brought on-line rapidly and has
    sufficient capacity to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} operate the handling system for any skip, diving
      bell, diving submersible or ADS used in the diving operation,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} heat any compression chamber used in the diving
      operation and heat, for the period required to complete the diving
      operation, any diver who is involved in the diving operation and
      who is in the water,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} sustain the life-support system of any
      compression chamber used in the diving operation and of any diver
      who makes a dive that is part of the diving operation,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} illuminate the interior of any compression
      chamber used in the diving operation, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} operate any communication system and monitoring
      system used in the diving operation; and

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} where any craft is used in the diving operation,
    there is provided a safe means of ensuring that the craft is, during
    the diving operation,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at anchor,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} made fast to the shore or to an installation,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} maintained in position using its propulsion
      system in accordance with section 25, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} used in such a manner as the Minister determines
      pursuant to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act or as approved in
      accordance with section 5 for the diving program of which the
      diving operation is a part.

### [Communication Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916849 .Subheading}

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no diving
  contractor shall conduct a diving operation unless there is available
  for use in the diving operation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for communications between the supervisor of the
    diving operation and any diver or pilot involved in the diving
    operation

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a primary communication system that has

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} sound reproduction adequate to enable breathing
        to be clearly heard and oral communications to be clearly heard
        and understandable, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a recording device that continuously records
        all oral communications while a dive is in progress, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a secondary communication system that allows the
      supervisor and the divers or pilots to communicate orally in the
      event of a failure of the primary communication system; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for communications between the supervisor and any
    person involved in, or in a position to assist in, the diving
    operation, other than the divers and pilots referred to in paragraph
    (a), a communication system that meets the requirements of paragraph
    6(1)(f).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a diving operation
  where SCUBA is used and where it is impracticable to use the
  communication systems referred to in that subsection, in which case no
  diving contractor shall conduct such a diving operation unless there
  is available for use in the diving operation an alternative method of
  communication that is adequate for the type of diving operation and
  that the supervisor of the diving operation considers suitable for the
  diving operation.

### [Pressure Vessels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916859 .Subheading}

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** No diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use or permit to be used in the diving
operation a pressure vessel intended for human occupancy unless the
pressure vessel is equipped with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a breathing mask for each occupant of the pressure
  vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a means of maintaining the oxygen, carbon dioxide,
  temperature and humidity in the pressure vessel at levels and
  pressures that are safe for the occupants; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for use in an emergency, a back-up capability to
  maintain the levels and pressures referred to in paragraph (b) for a
  minimum of, in the case of a diving bell or the compression chamber of
  a diving submersible, 24 hours and, in any other case, 48 hours.

### [Compression Chambers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916864 .Subheading}

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** No diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use or permit to be used a compression
chamber in the diving operation unless the compression chamber

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of section 14;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is designed and constructed in accordance with an
  acceptable standard;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} provides a suitable environment for its occupants,
  including amenities appropriate to the type, depth and duration of the
  diving operation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with doors that act as pressure seals and
  that can be opened from both the inside and the outside;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} is designed to minimize the risk of fire and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is constructed of only non-combustible or
    fire-resistant materials, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with suitable firefighting
    capabilities;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with adequate equipment, including
  facilities for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} supplying to and maintaining for its occupants an
    appropriate breathing mixture,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} lighting and heating the compression chamber, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} removing carbon dioxide;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with valves, gauges and other fittings
  necessary to indicate and control the internal pressures of each
  compartment from outside the compression chamber;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with piping that has at least one external
  shut-off valve, where practicable, immediately outside the point at
  which the piping enters the compression chamber and one internal
  shut-off valve immediately inside that point;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with hull integrity valves that clearly
  indicate whether the valves are in the open or closed position and
  that are clearly labelled by name and number;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} other than a diving bell and the compression chamber
  of a diving submersible, is equipped with a built-in breathing system
  that permits outside dumping of exhaled gas;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} where appropriate, is equipped with an emergency
  shut-off valve that automatically shuts off the flow of gas from the
  compression chamber if the velocity or volume of gas exceeds the
  preset limit;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with relief valves resistant to marine
  corrosion;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} has all of its internal electrical wiring insulated
  and in conduit, except for the wiring for low-power devices such as
  telephones;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} is cleaned and disinfected using only products that
  are

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} recommended by the manufacturer for that type of
    use,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} well proven for that purpose,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} non-toxic at any pressure,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} non-corrosive, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} safe to use;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} where used in a category II dive or a category III
  dive or, where practicable, in a category I dive, is provided with a
  coupling arrangement that is suitable for the safe transfer of persons
  under pressure and that is designed to prevent accidental release;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} is provided with a clamping mechanism that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is suitable for coupling a diving bell with the
    surface compression chamber,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} clearly indicates when the clamping mechanism is
    fully engaged, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} cannot be disengaged while pressurized;

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} is supplied with breathing mixture through a gas
  control panel that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has distinct indicators of the function of each
    valve and gauge, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is designed so as to minimize the possibility of
    supplying an incorrect breathing mixture;

- [(r)]{.lawlabel} if manufactured after December 31, 1990,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with a device to record continuously and
    to preserve at least the last recorded four hours of data respecting
    temperature, oxygen levels, depth, time and oral communications and,
    where practicable, carbon dioxide and humidity levels, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where practicable, is provided with a means to
    permit video monitoring of its occupants; and

- [(s)]{.lawlabel} if manufactured on or before December 31, 1990, meets
  the requirements set out in paragraph (r) where the Minister so
  determines pursuant to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act.

### [Surface Compression Chambers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916902 .Subheading}

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** No diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use or permit to be used a surface
compression chamber in the diving operation unless the surface
compression chamber

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of sections 14 and 15;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} contains at least two independently sealable
  compartments;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} contains sufficient space in at least one of its
  compartments to enable each occupant to lie down comfortably in the
  compartment;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} where a person will be in the surface compression
  chamber for a period of eight consecutive hours or less, has an
  internal vertical diameter of at least 1.5 m;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} where a person will be in the surface compression
  chamber for a period of more than eight consecutive hours, has an
  internal vertical diameter of at least 2 m;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with a medical lock;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} where the surface compression chamber will be used
  for a period of more than 12 consecutive hours, has adequate
  sanitation facilities;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} if manufactured after December 31, 1990, is fitted
  with a through-hull connector suitable for medical monitoring of its
  occupants; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} if manufactured on or before December 31, 1990, meets
  the requirements of paragraph (h) where the Minister so determines
  pursuant to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act.

### [Diving Bells]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916913 .Subheading}

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** No diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use or permit to be used a diving bell
in the diving operation unless the diving bell

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements of sections 14 and 15;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is equipped to permit the safe transfer of persons
  under pressure to and from a surface compression chamber;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is of a design that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} provides for an internal space of at least 2 m^3^
    for two-person occupancy and 3 m^3^ for three-person occupancy,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} enables divers to enter and exit without
    difficulty, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} allows at least two divers dressed-in for a
    diving operation to be seated comfortably therein;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with valves, gauges and other fittings
  necessary to control the internal pressure and to indicate both inside
  the diving bell and at the diving station the internal and external
  pressures;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} is so equipped that any valve used to pressurize the
  diving bell is spring-loaded so as to close when not held in the open
  position;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} contains adequate equipment, including reserve
  facilities, for supplying the appropriate breathing mixture to persons
  occupying or working from the diving bell, which reserve facilities
  shall be protected against inadvertent operation and be capable of
  being brought on-line from inside the diving bell without the
  assistance of any other person;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with a two-way oral communication system
  by means of which a person inside the diving bell can communicate with
  the diving supervisor of the diving operation and, via the diving
  supervisor, with other persons;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} contains equipment for lighting and heating the
  diving bell;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with suitable emergency life-support
  equipment and provisions for each occupant of the diving bell;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with a lifting device sufficient to
  enable an unconscious or injured diver to be hoisted into the diving
  bell by a person located in it;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} is provided with lifting equipment that enables the
  diving bell to be lowered to the depth at which the diving operation
  is to be conducted, to be maintained in its position and to be raised,
  all without excessive lateral, vertical or rotational movement;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} is provided with a means whereby, in the event of the
  failure of the lifting equipment referred to in paragraph (k), the
  diving bell can be returned to the surface and, where that means
  involves the shedding of weights, the controls for that shedding can
  be operated from within the diving bell, and a means is incorporated
  to prevent the accidental shedding of those weights;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} in addition to a primary lifting cable, is equipped
  with a suitable tag rope so designed that, in the event the primary
  cable breaks during a water-air interface transport, the tag rope will
  not permit the diving bell to descend to a depth greater than 25 m;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with a secondary lifting eye, or similar
  device that is of at least the same strength as the primary lifting
  eye, and is provided with a secondary lifting cable that is readily
  available and that has at least the same strength as the primary
  lifting cable and is compatible with the secondary lifting eye or
  similar device;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with equipment to enable occupants of the
  diving bell to monitor the temperature, oxygen and carbon dioxide
  levels within the diving bell;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} is equipped with a stroboscopic light that is
  automatically activated in the water and a pinger that operates at a
  frequency of 37.5 kHz;

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister so determines pursuant to
  paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act, is fitted with a relocation
  transponder system that operates at a frequency of 37.5 kHz;

- [(r)]{.lawlabel} where appropriate, is fitted with hull integrity
  valves and non-return valves on all gas and, where practicable,
  hot-water circuits connected to the diving bell;

- [(s)]{.lawlabel} if manufactured after December 31, 1990,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is so designed that, if necessary, a diver within
    the diving bell can

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} disconnect or shear the primary lifting cable and
      the umbilical,

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} disconnect or shear any other attachments that
      might prevent ascent,

    - [(C)]{.lawlabel} start, accelerate, decelerate or stop the ascent,
      and

    - [(D)]{.lawlabel} where practicable, start, accelerate, decelerate
      or stop the descent, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with a device to record continuously and
    to preserve at least the last recorded four hours of data respecting
    temperature, oxygen levels, depth, time, oral communications,
    internal and external ambient pressure, and the quantity of
    breathing mixture and electrical power available for use in an
    emergency and, where practicable, carbon dioxide and humidity
    levels; and

- [(t)]{.lawlabel} if manufactured on or before December 31, 1990, meets
  the requirements set out in paragraph (s) where the Minister so
  determines pursuant to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act.

### [Diving Submersibles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916944 .Subheading}

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** No diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use or permit to be used a diving
submersible in the diving operation unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the compression chamber of the diving submersible
  meets the requirements of sections 14, 15 and 17, except paragraphs
  17(k) to (n) and subparagraph 17(s)(i);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} during any period in which the diving submersible is
  in use, it is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} resting on the bottom,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} firmly secured at or near the work site where it
