    is to be used, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} secured in such a manner as the Minister
    determines pursuant to paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act or as approved
    in accordance with section 5 for the diving program of which the
    diving operation is a part;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is a means of maintaining at a safe level the
  body temperature of a person in the compression chamber of the diving
  submersible and a person in the water making a dive from the diving
  submersible; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a diver is present in the compression chamber of the
  diving submersible at any time that a dive is in progress.

### [Oxygen Supply Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916953 .Subheading}

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  an oxygen supply system is to be used in a diving operation, the
  diving contractor who conducts the diving operation shall use or
  permit to be used only an oxygen supply system the design of which
  complies with the requirements that:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the use of hoses and piping be kept to a minimum;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the materials used be compatible with oxygen at the
    pressures and temperatures for which the oxygen supply system is
    designed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the possibility of contamination of the oxygen by
    other gases, and vice versa, be minimized;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} high-velocity flows of oxygen be avoided;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the differential pressure throughout the oxygen
    supply system be kept as low as is practicable; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} quick-shut-off valves not be installed in the
    oxygen supply system except for one-quarter-turn valves that are
    connected to lines with reduced oxygen pressure and that may be used
    in an emergency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A diving contractor who conducts a diving operation
  shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any oxygen storage area for the diving operation is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} adequately ventilated,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} properly identified with warning signs,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} equipped with a fire suppression system,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} kept clear of and located as far as practicable
      away from combustible materials, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} if located in an enclosed area, equipped with an
      oxygen detector and an alarm designed to give warning of oxygen
      levels in excess of the concentration of oxygen in the ambient
      air;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person responsible for handling or otherwise
    dealing with oxygen is specially trained in that work; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} oxygen is transferred using only pumps, compressors
    or pressure differential systems that are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} recommended for that purpose by the manufacturer,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} operated in accordance with the manufacturer's
      instructions, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} operated by a person authorized to do so by the
      supervisor of the diving operation.

### [Breathing Mixture Supply Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916974 .Subheading}

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** A diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use or permit to be used in the diving
operation only a breathing mixture supply system that is so designed
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any interruption of the supply of breathing mixture
  to a person will not affect in any manner the supply of breathing
  mixture to any other person; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any failure of the primary supply of breathing
  mixture to a person will not affect in any manner the supply of
  breathing mixture to that person from that person's bale-out gas
  bottle or from the reserve referred to in subparagraph 22(1)(a)(ii).

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** No diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use in the diving operation an on-line
gas blender or diver's gas recovery system unless, at all times that the
blender or recovery system is in use,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is a buffer tank in use downstream of the
  blender or recovery system, as the case may be;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the blended breathing mixture is constantly analysed
  for its oxygen content; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the quantity, referred to in clause 22(1)(a)(iii)(C),
  of appropriate breathing mixture bypassing, in an emergency, the
  blender or recovery system, as the case may be, is available for
  immediate use.

### [Quantity and Quality of Breathing Mixture]{.HTitleText2} {#h-916982 .Subheading}

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  diving contractor who conducts a diving operation shall conduct or
  permit the commencement or continuation of the diving operation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total quantity of appropriate breathing mixture
    that is available at any time during the diving operation consists
    of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an adequate quantity to complete the diving
      operation,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a reasonable quantity for a reserve supply, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for use in an emergency, an additional supply
      that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving operation in which a
        diving bell is used, a sufficient quantity to meet the needs of
        the occupants of the diving bell for a minimum of 24 hours,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving operation in which an
        ADS is used, a sufficient quantity to meet the needs of the
        occupants of the ADS for a minimum of 48 hours,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving operation in which an
        on-line gas blender or diver's gas recovery system is used, a
        sufficient quantity to allow the divers to continue, interrupt
        or discontinue the diving operation safely, and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving operation in which a
        surface compression chamber is used, a quantity that is twice
        the amount required to pressurize the surface compression
        chamber to a pressure equivalent to the pressure at the greatest
        depth in respect of which the surface compression chamber will
        be used in the diving operation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the purity of the breathing mixture is of an
    acceptable standard; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the quantities referred to in subparagraphs (a)(ii)
    and (iii) are available for immediate use at a flow rate,
    temperature and pressure that are safe for the user.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No diving contractor shall conduct a diving operation
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any breathing mixture to be used in the diving
    operation is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} analysed for the accuracy of its oxygen content
      and, where practicable, its other contents immediately prior to
      the commencement of the dive that is part of the diving operation,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} supplied at temperature and humidity levels that
      are safe; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the levels of oxygen and carbon dioxide in the
    breathing mixture to be used in the diving operation are maintained
    at levels that are suitable for the type, depth and duration of the
    diving operation.

### [Evacuation, Rescue and Treatment Facilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917000 .Subheading}

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  diving contractor who conducts a diving operation shall provide for
  the availability of evacuation, rescue and treatment facilities and
  devices that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are suitable for the type, depth and duration of
    the diving operation and for the environmental conditions under
    which the diving operation is conducted; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have been approved in accordance with section 5 for
    the diving program of which the diving operation is a part.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The evacuation, rescue and treatment facilities and
  devices referred to in subsection (1) shall be available

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for use by persons involved in the diving operation
    as quickly as possible and within the period of time for which the
    life-support system of the surface compression chamber, diving bell
    or ADS used in the diving operation is capable of maintaining the
    life of the occupants; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where practicable, on site.

### [Medical Services]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917008 .Subheading}

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** A diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that at all times during the diving operation
  each diving crew involved in the diving operation includes a
  hyperbaric first-aid technician available on the craft or installation
  from which the diving operation is conducted;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} arrange for the services, on a 24 hour a day basis,
  of a specialized diving doctor, referred to in paragraph 4(3)(d), who
  is familiar with the diving procedures to be used in the diving
  operation and who is within a travelling distance of the diving
  operation that is acceptable to the Minister, to provide medical
  assistance in the event of an emergency;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that an adequate means of communication exists
  on a 24 hour a day basis between

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the diving station, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the craft or installation from which the diving
    operation is being conducted

  and the specialized diving doctor referred to in paragraph (b); and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} locate the nearest surface compression chamber that
  is compatible with the equipment used in the diving operation and that
  is suitable for the type, depth and duration of the diving operation
  to be conducted and shall make arrangements for the use of that
  surface compression chamber in the event of an emergency.

### [Craft in Dynamically Positioned Mode]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917017 .Subheading}

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** No diving contractor who
conducts a diving operation shall use or permit to be used a craft in
the dynamically positioned mode in the diving operation unless that use
was specifically approved in accordance with section 5 for the diving
program of which the diving operation is a part and the following
requirements are complied with:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the craft is so designed and constructed that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than one prime mover is available for each
    fore, aft and thwartship thruster,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the event of the failure of any prime mover or
    manoeuvring unit of the craft, the position of the craft can be
    maintained during the period it would take for the safe recovery of
    divers,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the arrangement of the thrusters and their size
    and number enable, in the event of the loss of any thruster of the
    craft, the heading and the position of the craft to be maintained
    within the environmental and operational capacity limits of that
    craft for the time it takes to safely recover any skip, diving bell
    or ADS used in the diving operation,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} for each manoeuvring unit necessary to maintain
    the craft in the dynamically positioned mode, other than the
    propellers and energy plant units, there is a reserve duplicate unit
    and an automatic and a manual system to switch from the on-line unit
    to the duplicate unit,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor on duty at the diving station on the
    craft can, by means of an alarm system connecting the bridge of the
    craft to the diving station on the craft, be kept informed by the
    person who controls the dynamic positioning system of any
    station-keeping problems or any other problems that might affect the
    safety of the diving operation,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a computer system controls the dynamic positioning
    of the craft and another independent, duplicate computer system is
    available to take over control automatically in the event of failure
    of the on-line computer system,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} there are on line at least two reference systems
    independently linked into each computer system referred to in
    subparagraph (vi),

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} during any time that a person involved in the diving
  operation is in the water

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a person who is responsible for the navigation of
    the craft and a person who is responsible for the control of the
    dynamic positioning system are in the control room of the craft,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the machinery spaces of the craft, except those in
    the pontoons of a semi-submersible craft, are manned, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in any one manoeuvre, the craft is not moved more
    than 5 m or the heading of the craft is not changed more than 5°,
    whichever is the lesser movement in relation to the location of the
    dive site of the diving operation; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person who is responsible for the control of the
  dynamic positioning system of the craft has at least six months
  experience using both the manual and the automatic modes of that
  particular system or, where that is impracticable, of a similar
  system, and at least two weeks briefing by the designer or
  manufacturer of the craft on the behaviour and hydrodynamics of that
  craft when operating in the dynamically positioned mode.

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Diving Safety Specialists]{.HTitleText1} {#h-917032 .Part}

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  operator, pursuant to paragraph 6(1)(a), or diving contractor,
  pursuant to subsection 9(1), shall engage the services of a person as
  a diving safety specialist unless the person holds a diving
  supervisor's certificate that is issued pursuant to section 71 and
  that is appropriate to the category of dive in respect of which that
  person will be giving advice and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has passed a test that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is acceptable to the Minister; and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} indicates that the person has an adequate
      knowledge of the safety, personnel, technical, operational,
      management, marketing and regulatory aspects of diving operations
      appropriate to the category of diving supervisor's certificate
      that person holds; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's knowledge is equivalent to the knowledge described
    in paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been engaged as a diving safety
  specialist for a diving program by an operator, pursuant to paragraph
  6(1)(a), shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} advise the operator on all safety aspects of the
    diving program including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the application for authorization, under
      paragraph 3.2(1)(b) of the Act, for the diving program,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any application made by the operator for
      authorization pursuant to subsection 12.2(1) of the Act, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any decision by the operator to interrupt or
      discontinue the diving program or any portion of the diving
      program for safety reasons; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be available on a 24 hour a day basis to advise any
    person involved in the diving program, including persons making
    decisions affecting the safety of divers involved in the diving
    program, on the safety aspects of the diving program.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been engaged as a diving safety
  specialist for a diving operation by a diving contractor, pursuant to
  subsection 9(1), shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} advise the diving contractor on all safety aspects
    of the diving operation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be available on a 24 hour a day basis to advise any
    person involved in the diving operation, including any person making
    decisions affecting the safety of divers involved in the diving
    operation, on all safety aspects of the diving operation.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A diving safety specialist referred to in
  subsection (2) or (3) shall, in advising any person pursuant to those
  subsections, take into account as a primary consideration the safety
  of any divers involved in the diving program or diving operation, as
  the case may be.

## [PART V]{.HLabel1}[Supervisors]{.HTitleText1} {#h-917049 .Part}

### [Supervision of a Category I Diving Operation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917050 .Subheading}

**[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** No person shall supervise
a category I diving operation unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has been appointed in writing pursuant to paragraph
  9(3)(a);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been certified to be medically fit

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to dive, in accordance with paragraph 53(b), or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to supervise by a medical doctor who has examined
    the person not more than 12 months prior to the date on which the
    diving operation is to be conducted and who has recorded the results
    of the examination on a medical examination record in the form set
    out in Schedule V, or another form acceptable to the Minister, and
    in a diving supervisor's medical certificate in that person's
    supervisor's logbook, referred to in section 51;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid diving supervisor's certificate issued
  pursuant to section 28, 30, 32 or 71, or a valid document that is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the basis
    of training and experience that are equivalent to the training and
    experience required of a person to obtain a certificate pursuant to
    section 28, 30 or 32, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} has satisfied the diving contractor who conducts the
  diving operation that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person has sufficient diving and supervisory
    experience and adequate knowledge in the use of the diving plant and
    equipment to be used in the diving operation, or a similar type of
    diving plant and equipment, and the breathing mixture to be used in
    the diving operation, and is familiar with the relevant provisions
    of the procedures manual referred to in paragraph 4(4)(a) and the
    contingency plan referred to in paragraph 4(4)(i) to be used in the
    diving operation, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's involvement in the diving operation
    is in no way contrary to any restriction, pursuant to section 35, in
    that person's supervisor's certificate or attached to that person's
    document referred to in paragraph (c).

### [Category I Diving Supervisor's Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917062 .Subheading}

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a category I diving supervisor's
  certificate that is valid for one year to a person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} been, for at least three years, the holder of a
      category I diving certificate issued pursuant to section 54 or 71,
      or a document that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the
        basis of training and experience that are equivalent to the
        training and experience required of a person to obtain a
        certificate pursuant to section 54, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} been, during the 12 months preceding the
      application, an assistant diving supervisor for at least 16
      category I dives and has supervised at least two real or mock
      incidents involving decompression sickness,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} submitted to the Minister a letter of
      recommendation that is in the form set out in Schedule VI and is
      signed by a diving contractor or operator and by a diving
      supervisor, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} passed a test acceptable to the Minister for a
      category I diving supervisor's certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a category I diving supervisor's certificate
    that was issued pursuant to this subsection but is no longer valid
    because it was not renewed pursuant to subsection (2) and who meets
    the requirements of subparagraphs (a)(ii) and (iii); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience required of a person to obtain a certificate
    pursuant to paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of a
  category I diving supervisor's certificate issued pursuant to
  subsection (1), renew the certificate for a period of one year where
  the holder of that certificate has supervised at least 12 category I
  dives and at least two real or mock incidents involving decompression
  sickness during the 12 months preceding the application.

### [Supervision of a Category II Diving Operation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917075 .Subheading}

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** No person shall supervise
a category II diving operation unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has been appointed in writing pursuant to paragraph
  9(3)(b);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} meets the criteria set out in paragraphs 27(b) and
  (d); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid diving supervisor's certificate issued
  pursuant to section 30, 32 or 71, or a valid document that is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the basis
    of training and experience that are equivalent to the training and
    experience required of a person to obtain a certificate pursuant to
    section 30 or 32, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister.

### [Category II Diving Supervisor's Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917082 .Subheading}

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a category II diving supervisor's
  certificate that is valid for one year to a person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} been, for at least two years, the holder of a
      category II diving certificate issued pursuant to section 56 or
      71, or a document that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the
        basis of training and experience that are equivalent to the
        training and experience required of a person to obtain a
        certificate pursuant to section 56, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} been, during the 12 months preceding the
      application, an assistant diving supervisor for at least six
      category II dives and 10 category I dives and has supervised at
      least two real or mock incidents involving decompression sickness,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} submitted to the Minister a letter of
      recommendation that is in the form set out in Schedule VI and is
      signed by a diving contractor or operator and by a diving
      supervisor who holds a category II or III diving supervisor's
      certificate, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} passed a test acceptable to the Minister for a
      category II diving supervisor's certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a category II diving supervisor's certificate
    that was issued pursuant to this subsection but is no longer valid
    because it was not renewed pursuant to subsection (2) and who meets
    the requirements of subparagraphs (a)(ii) and (iii); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience required of a person to obtain a certificate
    pursuant to paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of a
  category II diving supervisor's certificate issued pursuant to
  subsection (1), renew the certificate for a period of one year where
  the holder of that certificate has supervised at least 12 dives, of
  which at least six were category II dives, and has supervised at least
  two real or mock incidents involving decompression sickness during the
  12 months preceding the application.

### [Supervision of a Category III Diving Operation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917095 .Subheading}

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** No person shall supervise
a category III diving operation unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has been appointed in writing pursuant to paragraph
  9(3)(c);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} meets the criteria set out in paragraphs 27(b) and
  (d); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid category III diving supervisor's
  certificate issued pursuant to section 32 or 71, or a valid document
  that is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the basis
    of training and experience that is equivalent to the training and
    experience required of a person to obtain a certificate pursuant to
    section 32, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister.

### [Category III Diving Supervisor's Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917102 .Subheading}

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a category III diving supervisor's
  certificate that is valid for one year to a person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} been, for at least two years, the holder of a
      category III diving certificate issued pursuant to section 58 or
      71, or a document that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the
        basis of training and experience that are equivalent to the
        training and experience required of a person to obtain a
        certificate pursuant to section 58, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} been, during the 12 months preceding the
      application, an assistant diving supervisor for at least 16 dives
      of which at least two were saturation dives and six were category
      II dives and has supervised at least two real or mock incidents
      involving decompression sickness,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} submitted to the Minister a letter of
      recommendation that is in the form set out in Schedule VI and is
      signed by a diving contractor or operator and by two diving
      supervisors each of whom holds a category III diving supervisor's
      certificate, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} passed a test acceptable to the Minister for a
      category III diving supervisor's certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a category III diving supervisor's certificate
    that was issued pursuant to this subsection but is no longer valid
    because it was not renewed pursuant to subsection (2) and who meets
    the requirements of subparagraphs (a)(ii) and (iii); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience required of a person to obtain a certificate
    pursuant to paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of a
  category III diving supervisor's certificate issued pursuant to
  subsection (1), renew the certificate for a period of one year where
  the holder of that certificate has supervised at least 12 dives of
  which at least one was a saturation dive and at least six were
  category II dives and has supervised at least two real or mock
  incidents involving decompression sickness during the 12 months
  preceding the application.

### [Supervision of an ADS Diving Operation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917115 .Subheading}

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** No person shall supervise
an ADS diving operation unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has been appointed in writing pursuant to paragraph
  9(3)(d);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} meets the criteria set out in paragraph 27(b) and
  subparagraph 27(d)(ii);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} has satisfied the diving contractor who conducts the
  diving operation that the person has sufficient pilot and ADS
  supervisory experience and adequate knowledge in the use of the type
  of ADS to be used in the diving operation and is familiar with the
  relevant provisions of the procedures manual referred to in paragraph
  4(4)(a) and the contingency plan referred to in paragraph 4(4)(i) to
  be used in the diving operation; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid ADS supervisor's certificate issued
  pursuant to section 34 or 71, or a valid document that is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the basis
    of training and experience that are equivalent to the training and
    experience required of a person to obtain a certificate pursuant to
    section 34, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister.

### [ADS Supervisor's Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917123 .Subheading}

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue an ADS supervisor's certificate
  that is valid for one year to a person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} been, for at least three years, the holder of a
      pilot's certificate issued pursuant to section 65 or 71, or a
      document that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the
        basis of training and experience that is equivalent to the
        training and experience required of a person to obtain a
        certificate pursuant to section 65, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} made at least 20 ADS dives with a total bottom
      time of at least 80 hours, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} submitted to the Minister a letter of
      recommendation that is signed by a diving contractor or operator
      and by an ADS supervisor and that is acceptable to the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held an ADS supervisor's certificate that was
    issued pursuant to this subsection but is no longer valid because it
    was not renewed pursuant to subsection (2) and has supervised at
    least 10 ADS dives with a total bottom time of at least 25 hours
    during the 12 months preceding the application; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience required of a person to obtain a certificate
    pursuant to paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of an
  ADS supervisor's certificate issued pursuant to subsection (1), renew
  the certificate for a period of one year where the holder of the
  certificate has supervised at least six ADS dives with a total bottom
  time of at least 20 hours during the 12 months preceding the
  application.

### [Restrictions Respecting Supervisor's Certificate and Document]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917135 .Subheading}

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may insert in a supervisor's certificate issued pursuant to
  section 28, 30, 32, 34 or 71, or attach to a document referred to in
  subparagraph 28(1)(a)(i), 30(1)(a)(i), 32(1)(a)(i) or 34(1)(a)(i),
  restrictions with respect to the supervision of a diving operation by
  the holders of the certificate or the document where the Minister
  considers such restrictions necessary for safety reasons.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister inserts a restriction in a
  certificate or attaches a restriction to a document, pursuant to
  subsection (1), the Minister shall give the holder of the certificate
  or the document, as the case may be, an opportunity to show cause why
  the restriction should not be so inserted or attached.

### [Invalidation of Supervisor's Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917139 .Subheading}

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may invalidate a supervisor's certificate issued pursuant to
  section 28, 30, 32, 34 or 71 where, in the opinion of the Minister,
  the holder of the certificate has become incompetent or incapacitated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister proposes to invalidate a
  supervisor's certificate pursuant to subsection (1), the Minister
  shall give the holder of the certificate at least 30 days' notice in
  writing setting out the reasons for the proposed invalidation and
  shall give the holder an opportunity to show cause why the certificate
  should not be invalidated.

### [Duties of Supervisors]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917143 .Subheading}

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  diving supervisor shall, in a diving operation supervised by the
  diving supervisor, permit a person to make

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a category I dive in the diving operation unless
    the person meets the criteria set out in section 53, 55 or 57;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a category II dive in the diving operation unless
    the person meets the criteria set out in section 55 or 57; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a category III dive in the diving operation unless
    the person meets the criteria set out in section 57.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No ADS supervisor shall, in an ADS diving operation
  supervised by the ADS supervisor, permit a person to make an ADS dive
  in the diving operation unless the person meets the criteria set out
  in section 64.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No supervisor shall, in a diving operation under his
  supervision, permit a person to be involved in the diving operation
  where the supervisor believes on reasonable grounds that the person is
  unfit to be involved in that diving operation or that the involvement
  of that person in the diving operation could compromise the safety of
  other persons involved in the diving operation.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A diving supervisor shall plan dives in such a manner
  that the total bottom time of a diver supervised by the supervisor
  does not exceed, in any 24 hour period,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a category I dive

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} five hours at depths of 20 m or less, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three hours at depths of more than 20 m;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a category II dive, three hours; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a category III dive, eight hours.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A diving supervisor shall plan a diving operation in
  such a manner that, where practicable, a diving bell is used in the
  diving operation for any dive to a depth of more than 30 m that
  requires surface decompression.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} An ADS supervisor shall ensure that a pilot
  supervised by the supervisor does not remain underwater, in any 24
  hour period, for more than a total of eight hours.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall ensure that, following a dive
  under his supervision, the diver or pilot who made the dive has an
  adequate rest period.

<!-- -->

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  supervisor of a diving operation shall be present at the diving
  station from which the diving operation is controlled at all times
  during the diving operation or during the period in which the
  supervisor is on duty, as the case may be, and shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} directly control the diving operation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use, during the total dive time of the diving
    operation, a sufficient number of trained persons to operate the
    diving plant and equipment used in that diving operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} follow the relevant provisions of the applicable
    procedures manual for that diving operation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision of these
  Regulations, the supervisor of a diving operation may, in the case of
  an emergency, allow or direct the use of diving techniques, equipment
  and procedures not permitted by these Regulations where that use
  provides the only available practicable means of ensuring or enhancing
  the safety of the persons involved in the diving operation.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The supervisor of a diving operation shall interrupt
  or discontinue the diving operation where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} continuation of the diving operation would or is
    likely to compromise the safety of any person involved in the diving
    operation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the water currents at the underwater work site of
    the diving operation are likely to compromise the safety of a diver
    or pilot involved in the diving operation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} combustible material is stored too close for safety
    to any diving plant and equipment used in the diving operation.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The supervisor of a diving operation that involves
  the use of a diving submersible shall, where practicable, discontinue
  the diving operation where the unused stored electrical power of the
  diving submersible reaches 20 per cent of the electrical power
  capacity of the diving submersible, excluding the back-up capability
  referred to in paragraph 14(c).

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the supervisor of a diving operation wishes to
  commence or continue the diving operation and the person in charge of
  the craft or installation from which the diving operation is being
  conducted considers that the commencement or continuation of the
  diving operation would compromise the safety of any person on the
  craft or installation or the safety of the craft or installation, the
  decision of the person in charge of the craft or installation
  respecting the commencement or continuation of the diving operation
  shall overrule the supervisor's decision.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} In the event of an accident, the supervisor of the
  diving operation shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} take such measures as are necessary to provide
    treatment to any person injured in the accident and to ensure the
    safety of the persons involved in the diving operation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} interrupt the diving operation or any portion of
    the diving operation that may have caused or contributed to the
    accident until the diving operation or portion of the diving
    operation can be safely resumed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} deliver the diving operations logbook referred to
    in paragraph 9(5)(m) to the operator responsible for the diving
    operation as soon as possible after the accident;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} keep the site of the accident undisturbed until a
    conservation engineer has completed inspection of the site;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} prepare a written report that contains a
    description of the accident, a summary of the events that led to the
    accident and the measures taken following the accident; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} deliver to the operator responsible for the diving
    operation the report referred to in paragraph (e).

<!-- -->

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  supervisor shall conduct a diving operation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prior to the commencement of a dive that is part of
    the diving operation, the supervisor has consulted the person in
    charge of the craft or installation from which the diving operation
    will be conducted and any other person whose assistance the
    supervisor considers necessary for the dive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor has taken into account, in any
    decision respecting the diving operation, the meteorological data
    available to the supervisor and the environmental conditions in the
    area of the proposed dive site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} protective headgear is available for any diver
    involved in the diving operation at any time that the diver is at or
    below the surface of the water and, where practicable, at any time
    the diver is transported in a skip;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} during any period of darkness or low visibility

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any diver involved in the diving operation is
      provided with, and has attached to the diver's person, a lamp or
      other suitable device that indicates the diver's location, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the nature of the diving operation
      permits, the dive site and the underwater work site of the diving
      operation is adequately illuminated;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any standby diver involved in the diving operation
    has an umbilical at least 3 m longer than the umbilical of the diver
    for whom the stand-by diver acts as stand-by;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the divers and pilots involved in the diving
    operation are protected from any danger or hazards that could be
    caused by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} sonar,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} devices emitting electromagnetic or ionizing
      radiation,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the propeller and the manoeuvring unit of any
      craft from which the diving operation is being conducted and the
      flows of water created by the propeller and the manoeuvering unit,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the normal movements of a craft referred to in
      subparagraph (iii) and any movements of the craft caused by
      unexpected loss of power or stability,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} any suction or water current encountered in or
      resulting from the diving operation, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} equipment on a craft or an installation from
      which the diving operation is being conducted; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} plans have been made, in the event a craft from
    which the diving operation is being conducted loses power, to
    protect and to recover a diver or pilot involved in the diving
    operation who is in the water.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No diving supervisor shall permit a diver supervised
  by the supervisor to enter the water unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the diver

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is wearing a diving harness complete with a
      pelvic support and lifting ring and is equipped, where
      practicable, with a depth indicator capable of being monitored
      from the surface, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has a bale-out gas bottle that is independent of
      the primary supply of breathing mixture to the diver; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all impressed current cathodic protection devices
    situated within a radius of 5 m from the diver's underwater work
    site are deactivated and the notice referred to in subparagraph
    6(1)(g)(ii) is prominently displayed on the controls of those
    devices, or other equally effective measures are taken to ensure the
    safety of any diver within a radius of 5 m of any active impressed
    current cathodic protection devices.

### [Restrictions Respecting Dive Sites]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917201 .Subheading}

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  diving supervisor shall permit a diver supervised by the supervisor to
  make a dive that is part of a diving operation from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a place referred to in paragraph 6(1)(b) that is
    unsuitable;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a craft that has insufficient power or stability
    for the safe conduct of the dive;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a dive site located more than 2 m above the water
    unless a suitable skip, diving bell or diving submersible is used to
    transport the diver through the air-water interface;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a dynamically positioned craft unless

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the craft has been operating in the dynamically
      positioned mode for at least 30 minutes before the diver enters
      the water,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the range of surge or sway movement of the water
      at the dive site is less than 80 per cent of the maximum
      operational capacity limit of the craft,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a skip or a diving bell is positioned as close
      as possible to the diver's underwater work site,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} all reasonable precautions are taken to prevent
      any umbilical used in the dive from coming into contact with any
      propeller or manoeuvring unit of the craft,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} any change of heading or positioning of the
      craft, at any time a diver involved in the diving operation is in
      the water, is made only after the diving supervisor has granted
      permission for the change and the diver has been notified, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the craft complies with the requirements of
      section 25; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a craft that is under way, except in the case of an
    emergency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a craft that is
  operating in the dynamically positioned mode and that complies with
  the requirements of section 25 is not considered to be under way.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No supervisor shall conduct a diving operation unless
  the person in charge of the craft or installation from which the
  diving operation is to be conducted has been notified of the proposed
  diving operation.

### [Restricted Use of SCUBA]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917217 .Subheading}

**[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** No diving supervisor
shall use or permit to be used SCUBA in a diving operation supervised by
the supervisor except where any other diving technique is impracticable
or hazardous to use and unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the diving operation is conducted in water that is
  less than 20 m deep;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the diving operation can be completed without the
  need for decompression;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the diver using SCUBA is connected to a lifeline or,
  where the use of a lifeline is impracticable,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the diver is in contact, visually or orally, with
    another diver who is in the water, securely connected to a lifeline
    and assisted by an attendant at the dive site, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} some other effective method of ensuring that
    diver's safety is provided;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} there is a practical means of communication between
  the supervisor and the diver using SCUBA and there is a means of oral
  communication between the supervisor and other personnel involved in
  the diving operation;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the diving crew for the duration of the diving
  operation includes a minimum of one supervisor, one diver, one
  stand-by diver and as many attendants as the supervisor considers
  necessary to ensure the safety of the divers involved in the diving
  operation; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} all applicable provisions of these Regulations are
  complied with.

### [Restrictions Respecting Category I Diving Operations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917227 .Subheading}

**[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** No diving supervisor
shall conduct a category I diving operation, other than a diving
operation in which SCUBA is used, unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a suitable skip is used to transport the divers
  involved in the diving operation to an underwater work site that is 20
  m or more in depth and, where practicable, to an underwater work site
  that is less than 20 m in depth;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an umbilical directly from the surface or via a skip
  is used to supply the appropriate breathing mixture to the divers
  involved in the dive that is part of the diving operation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor is in oral communication with any
  divers, stand-by divers and attendants involved in the diving
  operation at all times during the diving operation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor has a means of monitoring the depth of
  each diver involved in the diving operation and the pressure of the
  breathing mixture being supplied to each diver and stand-by diver
  involved in the dive;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} each diver involved in the dive is securely connected
  to a lifeline; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the diving crew for the duration of the diving
  operation, includes one diving supervisor, one diver and a minimum of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one stand-by diver equipped with an umbilical at
    least 3 m longer than the umbilical of the diver for whom the
    stand-by diver acts as stand-by,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one attendant at the dive site of the diving
    operation, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} as many additional attendants as the supervisor
    considers necessary to ensure the safety of the divers involved in
    the diving operation.

### [Restrictions Respecting Category II Diving Operations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917238 .Subheading}

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** No diving supervisor
shall conduct a category II diving operation unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements referred to in paragraphs 42(c)
  to (e) are complied with;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a diving bell or diving submersible is used for any
  descent or ascent of a diver to or from the underwater work site of
  the diving operation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the diving supervisor has a means of monitoring the
  internal pressure of any diving bell or surface compression chamber or
  the compression chamber of any diving submersible used in the diving
  operation; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the diving crew, for the duration of the diving
  operation, includes one diving supervisor and a minimum of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two divers who are in the diving bell or diving
    submersible used in the diving operation, one of whom is equipped
    with an umbilical at least 3 m longer than the umbilical of the
    diver for whom the stand-by diver acts as stand-by,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one additional stand-by diver and one attendant at
    the dive site of the diving operation, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} as many additional attendants as the supervisor
    considers necessary to ensure the safety of the divers involved in
    the diving operation.

### [Restrictions Respecting Category III Diving Operations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917247 .Subheading}

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  diving supervisor shall, in a saturation dive supervised by the
  supervisor, permit the total dive time of any diver involved in the
  dive to exceed 31 days.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No diving supervisor shall conduct a category III
  diving operation unless the diving crew, for the duration of the dive,
  includes the persons referred to in paragraph 43(d) and as many
  additional specialists and life-support technicians as the diving
  supervisor considers necessary to ensure the safety of the divers
  involved in the dive.

### [Restriction Respecting Diving Supervisors]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917251 .Subheading}

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** No diving supervisor
shall make a dive while supervising a diving operation, even in the case
of an emergency.

### [Additional Duties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917253 .Subheading}

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a skip, diving bell, diving submersible or ADS used in a diving
  operation is being lowered into or raised from the water, the
  supervisor of the diving operation shall ensure that the skip, diving
  bell, diving submersible or ADS, as the case may be, is continuously
  within the supervisor's vision, either directly or by any other means.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, in a diving operation, a diving bell is
  coupled with a surface compression chamber by means of a clamping
  mechanism, the supervisor of the diving operation shall permit only a
  person who is familiar with the operational procedures designed for
  the clamping mechanism to operate that clamping mechanism.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where, in a diving operation, a person is transferred
  to or from a diving bell, the supervisor of the diving operation shall
  ensure that any surface compression chambers used in the diving
  operation but not used in the transfer are, during the transfer,
  isolated from the surface compression chambers used in the transfer.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a diver involved in a diving operation exhibits
  any unusual psychological or physiological symptoms or any severe
  symptoms of decompression sickness, the diving supervisor of the
  diving operation shall advise the specialized diving doctor referred
  to in paragraph 4(3)(d) and the operator responsible for that diving
  operation of those symptoms and shall supervise any therapeutic
  recompression or decompression of the diver.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A diving supervisor shall take all reasonable
  precautions to ensure that, except in the event of the evacuation of a
  diver during a diving operation supervised by the diving supervisor,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a diver involved in the diving operation who has
    completed a dive does not fly in an aircraft

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for 12 hours following a non-decompression dive,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for 24 hours following decompression, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for such longer period as the diving supervisor
      considers necessary to ensure that the diver does not suffer
      decompression sickness; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a diver involved in the diving operation who has
    completed a saturation dive remains under observation in the general
    area of the decompression chamber for at least 24 hours after
    decompression or such longer period as is sufficient in the opinion
    of the diving supervisor to ensure the well-being of the diver.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A diving supervisor shall take all reasonable
  precautions to ensure that, in the evacuation of a person during a
  diving operation supervised by the supervisor, a person involved in
  the diving operation who has completed decompression within the
  preceding 24 hours does not fly in an aircraft at an altitude greater
  than is operationally necessary in the circumstances.

### [Diving Plant and Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917266 .Subheading}

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  supervisor shall conduct a diving operation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the diving plant and equipment referred to in
    paragraph 9(5)(h) meets the relevant requirements of sections 12 to
    21, is available for use when required and that diving plant and
    equipment, other than diving plant and equipment intended to be
    mobile during the diving operation, is, at all times during the
    diving operation, firmly secured to the craft or installation from
    which the diving operation is conducted; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any electrically operated diving plant and
    equipment that is used in the diving operation is suitable for the
    location in which it is to be used and is protected from hazards
    caused by water and environmental conditions.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No supervisor shall, in a diving operation supervised
  by the supervisor, use any diving plant and equipment in the diving
  operation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the appropriate examinations and tests referred to
    in subsection 11(1) have been carried out on the diving plant and
    equipment and the certificates related to those examinations and
    tests have been inserted into or attached to the register referred
    to in subsection 11(3); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor has, not more than 24 hours before
    such use,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} examined the diving plant and equipment in
      accordance with the relevant provisions of the applicable
      procedures manual and found it to be in good working order, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where appropriate, in addition to the
      examination referred to in subparagraph (i), tested for leaks any
      pump, compressor, cylinder or pipeline used in the diving
      operation to convey breathing mixture and found it free from
      leaks.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No diving supervisor shall conduct a dive unless a
  two-compartment compression chamber

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that has been approved in accordance with section 5
    for the diving program of which the dive is a part, to be used at a
    pressure that is not less than six atmospheres absolute and, where
    the maximum working pressure that may be encountered during the dive
    is greater than six atmospheres absolute, at the maximum working
    pressure plus one atmosphere, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is suitable for the dive

  is located in a readily accessible place on board the craft or
  installation from which the dive is conducted, except where the dive
  is conducted at a depth of 10 m or less, in which case the compression
  chamber may be located within one hour's travelling time from the dive
  site.

### [Oxygen Supply Systems and Breathing Mixture Supply Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917280 .Subheading}

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  supervisor shall conduct a diving operation in which

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an oxygen supply system is used unless the oxygen
    supply system meets the requirements set out in section 19;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a breathing mixture supply system is used unless
    the breathing mixture supply system meets the requirements set out
    in sections 20 and 21; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an analyser is used to determine the relative
    levels of oxygen and carbon dioxide during any dive that is part of
    the diving operation unless the analyser is recalibrated in
    accordance with the manufacturer's instructions for that analyser
    prior to the dive.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an analyser is used continuously in a diving
  operation to determine the relative levels of oxygen and carbon
  dioxide during any dive that is part of the diving operation, the
  supervisor of the dive shall ensure that the analyser is recalibrated
  in accordance with the manufacturer's instructions for that analyser,
  where practicable, every two hours.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No diving supervisor shall, in a diving operation
  supervised by the supervisor, use or permit to be used an on-line gas
  blender or a diver's gas recovery system in the diving operation
  unless throughout the period that the blender or the diver's gas
  recovery system is in use the requirements of section 21 are complied
  with.

### [Breathing Mixture]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917288 .Subheading}

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  supervisor shall commence or continue a diving operation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total quantity of appropriate breathing mixture
    that is available at any time during the diving operation consists
    of the quantities set out in section 22;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the purity of the breathing mixture is of an
    acceptable standard; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the quantities of breathing mixture referred to in
    subparagraphs 22(1)(a)(ii) and (iii) are available for immediate use
    at a flow rate, temperature and pressure that are safe for the user.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No supervisor shall permit a diver supervised by the
  supervisor to make a dive unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total quantity of appropriate breathing
    mixture, including the reserve supply,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} carried by the diver is sufficient to enable that
      diver to reach a skip, diving bell or diving submersible used in
      connection with the dive, a reserve supply referred to in
      subparagraph 22(1)(a)(ii) or the surface, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} available to the diver's stand-by diver for
      immediate use consists of an adequate quantity to enable the
      stand-by diver to reach the diver and to enable the stand-by diver
      and the diver

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} to carry out appropriate decompression
        procedures and return to the surface, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} to return to the skip, diving bell or diving
        submersible used in connection with the dive and to carry out
        appropriate decompression procedures either in that skip, diving
        bell or diving submersible, as the case may be, or at the
        surface; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor has analysed the breathing mixture
    for the accuracy of its oxygen content immediately prior to the
    dive.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No supervisor shall, in a diving operation supervised
  by the supervisor, use or permit to be used

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} compressed air as a breathing mixture at water
    depths greater than 50 m or at pressures that are equivalent to the
    pressures of water depths greater than 50 m except in the case of a
    category III dive; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pure oxygen as a breathing mixture except for
    decompression or therapeutic purposes.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall protect any breathing mixture to
  be used in a diving operation supervised by the supervisor from any
  likelihood of contamination.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a diving supervisor becomes aware of any oil or
  other contaminant in waters in which a diving operation supervised by
  the supervisor is being conducted, that supervisor shall take all
  necessary steps to avoid any contamination of any diver in the water
  and of the ambient atmosphere in any compression chamber used in the
  diving operation.

### [Diving Operations Logbooks]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917306 .Subheading}

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  supervisor shall enter in the diving operations logbook referred to in
  paragraph 9(5)(m), for each diving operation or portion of a diving
  operation supervised by the supervisor,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date and the time the diving operation was
    commenced and terminated including any time during which the diving
    operation was interrupted, or the date and the time at which the
    supervisor began the supervision and the time at which that
    supervision ended;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the diving contractor, if any, who
    conducted the diving operation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of the operator or the operator's
    representative responsible for the diving operation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name or other designation and the location of
    the craft or installation from which, or other dive site at which,
    the diving operation was conducted;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the identification number of any dive supervised
    during the diving operation or during the period of supervision
    referred to in paragraph (a);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the supervisor, the names of all other
    persons involved in the diving operation including those who
    operated any diving plant and equipment used in the diving
    operation, the names of the persons consulted pursuant to paragraph
    39(1)(a) and the names of any other persons consulted in respect of
    the diving operation and the positions or titles of all the persons
    named;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the procedures followed during the diving
    operation;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the decompression table and the schedule in that
    decompression table that were used in the diving operation;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the time at which any diver involved in the diving
    operation and any skip, diving bell, diving submersible or ADS used
    in the diving operation left the surface and returned to the
    surface;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the maximum depth, bottom time, dive time and total
    dive time for each dive conducted during the period of supervision
    referred to in paragraph (a);

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the type of diving plant and equipment and the type
    of breathing mixture used in the diving operation;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the type of discomfort, injury or illness,
    including decompression sickness, suffered by any person involved in
    the diving operation;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} the particulars of any environmental conditions
    that affected or might have affected the diving operation; and

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} any other factor relevant to the safety or health
    of any person involved in the diving operation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall, after completion of an entry in
  the diving operations logbook in accordance with subsection (1),
  immediately sign the entry and request the operator or the operator's
  representative responsible for the diving operation to countersign the
  entry as soon as possible.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall make any alteration to an entry in a
  diving operations logbook referred to in subsection (1) unless the
  alteration is initialled by the supervisor who made the entry and by
  the person who countersigned the entry.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The supervisor of a diving operation shall produce,
  on request, the diving operations logbook for the diving operation for
  inspection by a conservation engineer pursuant to paragraph 43(b) of
  the Act.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} At the time there is no space for further entries in
  a diving operations logbook for a diving operation, or at the time the
  diving operation is completed, whichever occurs first, the supervisor
  who made the last entry in the logbook shall deliver the logbook to
  the diving contractor who conducted the diving operation, but, in the
  event of an accident in connection with the diving operation, the
  supervisor on duty at the time of the accident shall deliver the
  logbook to the operator responsible for the diving operation as soon
  as possible after the accident.

### [Supervisor's Logbooks]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917327 .Subheading}

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  supervisor shall keep a logbook that is permanently bound, has
  numbered pages and contains the name and signature of the supervisor
  and a photograph that is a likeness of the supervisor.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall, as soon as possible after
  supervision of a dive or after a period of supervision of a portion of
  a dive, enter in the logbook referred to in subsection (1), for each
  dive or portion of a dive supervised by the supervisor,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of the dive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the diving contractor, if any, who
    conducted the dive;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of the operator or the operator's
    representative responsible for the diving operation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name or other designation and location of the
    craft or installation from which, or other dive site at which, the
    dive was conducted;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the dive identification number referred to in
    paragraph 50(1)(e);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of each diver or pilot supervised;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the maximum depth, bottom time and dive time of the
    dive;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the decompression table and the schedule in that
    decompression table that were used in the dive;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} details of any medical care or advice given and the
    type of therapeutic treatment used, if any;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} any emergency in connection with the dive; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} any other factor relevant to the safety or health
    of any person involved in the dive.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall, after completion of an entry in
  the supervisor's logbook in accordance with subsection (2),
  immediately sign the entry and request the operator or the operator's
  representative responsible for the dive to countersign the entry as
  soon as possible.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall make any alteration to an entry in a
  supervisor's logbook referred to in subsection (1) unless the
  alteration is initialled by the supervisor and by the person who
  countersigned the entry.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall produce, on request, the
  supervisor's logbook referred to in subsection (1) for inspection by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a conservation engineer pursuant to paragraph 43(b)
    of the Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the diving doctor who examines the supervisor for
    the purposes of these Regulations, at the time of the examination.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall keep in the supervisor's logbook
  referred to in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor's diving supervisor's certificate or
    ADS supervisor's certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the supervisor's written appointment as a
    supervisor pursuant to subsection 9(3);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any certificates or other evidence of qualification
    in addition to those referred to in paragraph (a); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any certificates or other evidence of medical
    examination received from a diving doctor.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A supervisor shall retain the supervisor's logbook
  referred to in subsection (1) for a period of not less than two years
  after the day of the last entry made in it.

### [Keeping of Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917353 .Subheading}

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person involved in a diving operation is in a compression chamber,
  the supervisor of the diving operation shall keep a record or ensure
  that a record is kept, at regular intervals of not more than 30
  minutes, of the time and the depth gauge readings and of the main
  components of the atmosphere in the compression chamber, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the oxygen and carbon dioxide; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the temperature and humidity.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The supervisor of a diving operation shall keep a
  record of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the results of any analyses of any breathing
    mixture used,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any scheduled and unscheduled maintenance performed
    on any component of the diving plant and equipment used, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the results of any readings taken pursuant to
    subsection (1),

  and shall keep a copy of any certifications and inspections carried
  out on the diving plant and equipment used.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} On completion of a diving operation, the supervisor
  of the diving operation shall deliver the records and copies kept
  pursuant to subsection (2) to the diving contractor who conducted the
  diving operation.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The supervisor of a diving operation shall make a
  tape recording of all communications between the divers or the pilots
  involved in a dive that is part of the diving operation and the
  supervisor during the pre-dive system check and during the dive and
  shall retain the tape recording for a minimum of 48 hours after the
  completion of the diving operation.

## [PART VI]{.HLabel1}[Divers]{.HTitleText1} {#h-917365 .Part}

### [Category I Dives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917366 .Subheading}

**[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** No person shall make a
category I dive in a diving operation unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is 18 years of age or older;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been certified to be medically fit to dive by a
  diving doctor who has

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} inspected the person's diver's logbook referred to
    in section 63,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} examined the person not more than 12 months prior
    to the period during which the diving operation is to be conducted,
    and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} recorded the results of the examination
    including, in the case of a person 35 years of age or older, the
    results of a stress ECG performance test on a treadmill or a
    bicycle, on a medical examination record in the form set out in
    Schedule VII or in another form acceptable to the Minister, and on a
    diver's medical certificate in the person's diver's logbook;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} has delivered a copy of the diver's medical
  certificate referred to in paragraph (b) to the diving contractor who
  conducts the diving operation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} holds

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a valid category I diving certificate issued
    pursuant to section 54 or 71,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} during the first year in which the person makes
    category I dives in a diving operation, a valid document that is

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the
      basis of training and experience that are equivalent to the
      training and experience referred to in paragraph 54(1)(a), and

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a valid category II diving certificate issued
    pursuant to section 56 or 71 or a valid document referred to in
    paragraph 55(1)(b), or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a valid category III diving certificate issued
    pursuant to section 58 or 71 or a valid document referred to in
    paragraph 57(1)(b); and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} has satisfied the supervisor of the diving operation
  that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person is capable of using, and has sufficient
    experience in the use of, the type of diving plant and equipment and
    breathing mixture to be used in the diving operation and is familiar
    with the relevant provisions of the procedures manual referred to in
    paragraph 4(4)(a) and the contingency plan referred to in paragraph
    4(4)(i) to be followed in the diving operation, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's involvement in the diving operation
    is in no way contrary to any restriction

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} inserted in that person's diving certificate or
      attached to that person's document referred to in paragraph (d)
      pursuant to section 59, or

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} inserted in that person's diver's medical
      certificate pursuant to section 60.

### [Category I Diving Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917386 .Subheading}

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a category I diving certificate
  that is valid for one year to a person who has attained a standard of
  competence in category I diving that is acceptable to the Minister and
  who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} holds a first-aid certificate acceptable to the
    Minister and has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} successfully completed, at a school, institution
      or company acceptable to the Minister, training in the theoretical
      and practical aspects of diving appropriate to category I diving,
      including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the use of air as a breathing mixture,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} surface-oriented diving techniques and
        operational procedures,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} diving techniques and operational procedures
        for use with SCUBA,

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} the use and operation of any diving plant and
        equipment, including hand-held tools,

      - [(E)]{.lawlabel} the use of communications systems,

      - [(F)]{.lawlabel} the use of decompression tables,

      - [(G)]{.lawlabel} emergency procedures, including hyperbaric
        first-aid techniques and the operation of surface compression
        chambers, and

      - [(H)]{.lawlabel} a thorough study of these Regulations, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} made at least 50 dives in various environmental
      conditions and locations and for various purposes with a bottom
      time totalling at least 50 hours, including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} at least 40 dives to depths of up to 20 m with
        a bottom time totalling at least 43 hours, of which at least 10
        were dives to depths of between 15 m and 20 m with a bottom time
        totalling at least seven hours, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} at least 10 dives to depths of between 20 m and
        50 m with a bottom time totalling at least seven hours, of which
        at least three hours were at depths of between 40 m and 50 m and
        at least one hour was at a depth of at least 50 m;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a category I diving certificate that was
    issued pursuant to this subsection but is no longer valid because it
    was not renewed pursuant to subsection (2) and who made at least 28
    dives with a bottom time totalling at least 24 hours during the 12
    months preceding the application;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience described in paragraph (a); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid document referred to in paragraph
    53(d).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of a
  category I diving certificate issued pursuant to subsection (1), renew
  the certificate for a period of one year where the holder of the
  certificate has made at least 24 category I dives with a bottom time
  totalling at least 20 hours during the 12 months preceding the
  application.

### [Category II Dives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917406 .Subheading}

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no person
  shall make a category II dive in a diving operation unless the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the criteria set out in paragraphs 53(a)
    to (c) and (e); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} holds

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a valid category II diving certificate issued
      pursuant to section 56 or 71,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} during the first year in which the person makes
      a category II dive in a diving operation, a valid document that is

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the
        basis of training and experience that are equivalent to the
        training and experience referred to in paragraph 56(1)(a), and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a valid category III diving certificate issued
      pursuant to section 58 or 71 or a valid document referred to in
      paragraph 57(1)(b).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A diver who holds a category I diving certificate may
  make a category II dive for training purposes in a diving operation
  where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the diver is employed in a diving program on a
    full-time basis to make category I dives;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the dive is authorized as a training dive by the
    operator or the operator's representative responsible for the diving
    operation and the person in charge of the craft or installation from
    which the dive will be conducted; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the diver makes the dive under the close
    supervision of a diver who holds a category II or category III
    diving certificate.

### [Category II Diving Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917420 .Subheading}

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a category II diving certificate
  that is valid for one year to a person who has attained a standard of
  competence in category II diving that is acceptable to the Minister
  and who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} successfully completed, at a school, institution
      or company acceptable to the Minister, training in the theoretical
      and practical aspects of diving appropriate to category II diving,
      including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the use of mixed gas as a breathing mixture,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} mixed gas diving techniques and operational
        procedures,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the use and operation of any diving plant and
        equipment,

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} any type of underwater work generally done by a
        diver,

      - [(E)]{.lawlabel} the use of communications systems,

      - [(F)]{.lawlabel} emergency procedures, including hyperbaric
        first-aid techniques and the operation of compression chambers,
        and

      - [(G)]{.lawlabel} a thorough study of these Regulations, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} been employed to make category I dives for at
      least the 12 months preceding the application and has made at
      least 60 dives in a diving bell or diving submersible with a
      bottom time totalling at least 20 hours, including at least 30
      lock-out dives of which four were to a depth of more than 50 m,
      two were to a depth of more than 80 m and one was to a depth of
      100 m or more, with a bottom time totalling at least 30 minutes
      per lock-out dive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a category II diving certificate that was
    issued pursuant to this subsection but is no longer valid because it
