    was not renewed pursuant to subsection (2) and who made at least 28
    dives with a bottom time totalling at least 24 hours of which at
    least 10 dives were category II dives with a bottom time totalling
    at least 10 hours during the 12 months preceding the application;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience described in paragraph (a); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid document referred to in paragraph
    55(1)(b).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of a
  category II diving certificate issued pursuant to subsection (1),
  renew the certificate for a period of one year where the holder of the
  certificate has made at least 24 dives with a bottom time totalling at
  least 20 hours, including at least eight category II dives with a
  bottom time totalling at least eight hours during the 12 months
  preceding the application.

### [Category III Dives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917437 .Subheading}

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no person
  shall make a category III dive in a diving operation unless the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the criteria set out in paragraphs 53(a)
    to (c) and (e); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid category III diving certificate
    issued pursuant to section 58 or 71 or, during the first year in
    which the person makes a category III dive in a diving operation, a
    valid document that is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the
      basis of training and experience that are equivalent to the
      training and experience described in paragraph 58(1)(a), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A diver who holds a category II diving certificate
  may make a category III dive for training purposes in a diving
  operation where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the diver is employed in a diving program on a
    full-time basis to make category II dives;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the dive is authorized as a training dive by the
    operator or the operator's representative responsible for the diving
    operation and the person in charge of the craft or installation from
    which the dive will be conducted; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the diver makes the dive under the close
    supervision of a diver who holds a category III diving certificate.

### [Category III Diving Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917448 .Subheading}

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a category III diving certificate
  that is valid for one year to a person who has attained a standard of
  competence in category III diving that is acceptable to the Minister
  and who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} successfully completed, at a school, institution
      or company acceptable to the Minister, training in the theoretical
      and practical aspects of diving appropriate to category III
      diving, including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} at least one saturation dive to a depth of not
        less than 75 m with at least two lock-out dives with a bottom
        time totalling at least 30 minutes per lock-out dive,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} saturation diving techniques and operational
        procedures,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the use and operation of any diving plant and
        equipment,

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} any type of underwater work generally done by a
        diver,

      - [(E)]{.lawlabel} emergency procedures relevant to saturation
        diving, including hyperbaric first-aid techniques and the
        operation of compression chambers, and

      - [(F)]{.lawlabel} a thorough study of these Regulations, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} been employed to make category II dives for at
      least the two years preceding the application and has made at
      least 24 category II dives;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a category III diving certificate that was
    issued pursuant to this subsection but is no longer valid because it
    was not renewed pursuant to subsection (2) and who made at least 28
    dives with a bottom time totalling at least 32 hours, including at
    least 10 category II dives with a bottom time totalling at least 10
    hours and at least one saturation dive during the 12 months
    preceding the application;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience referred to in paragraph (a); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid document referred to in paragraph
    57(1)(b).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of a
  category III diving certificate issued pursuant to subsection (1),
  renew the certificate for a period of one year where the holder of
  that certificate has made at least 24 dives with a bottom time
  totalling at least 24 hours, including at least eight category II
  dives from a diving bell or diving submersible with a bottom time
  totalling at least eight hours and at least one saturation dive during
  the 12 months preceding the application.

### [Restrictions Respecting Diving Certificates and Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917464 .Subheading}

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may insert in a diving certificate issued pursuant to section
  54, 56, 58 or 71, or attach to a document referred to in paragraph
  53(d), 55(1)(b) or 57(1)(b), restrictions on diving by the holder of
  the certificate or document where the Minister considers those
  restrictions necessary for safety reasons.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister inserts a restriction in a
  certificate or attaches a restriction to a document pursuant to
  subsection (1), the Minister shall give the holder of the certificate
  or document, an opportunity to show cause why that restriction should
  not be inserted or attached.

### [Restrictions Respecting Medical Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917468 .Subheading}

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  diving doctor who examines a diver for the purposes of paragraph 53(b)
  or section 71 may insert in the diver's medical certificate medical
  restrictions on diving by the holder of the medical certificate where
  the diving doctor considers those restrictions necessary for safety
  reasons.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a diving doctor inserts medical restrictions in
  a diver's medical certificate pursuant to subsection (1) or certifies
  in a medical certificate in a diver's logbook that the diver is
  medically unfit to dive, the Minister shall, on application by the
  diver within one month after the insertion or certification by the
  diving doctor, review that certificate and the diver's medical
  examination record related to the certificate with one or more
  specialized diving doctors.

### [Invalidation of Diving Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917472 .Subheading}

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may invalidate a diving certificate issued pursuant to
  section 54, 56, 58 or 71 where, in the opinion of the Minister, the
  holder of the certificate has become incompetent or incapacitated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister proposes to invalidate a diving
  certificate pursuant to subsection (1), the Minister shall give the
  holder of the certificate at least 30 days' notice in writing setting
  out the reasons for the proposed invalidation and shall give the
  holder an opportunity to show cause why the certificate should not be
  invalidated.

### [Diver's Duties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917476 .Subheading}

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no diver
  shall make a dive in a diving operation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prior to the dive, the diver has

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} checked the diver's personal diving equipment and
      is satisfied that the equipment is in good working order, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} reported to the supervisor of the diving
      operation any remedies, treatments, pharmaceuticals, intoxicants
      or drugs taken by the diver within the 48 hours preceding the
      dive, any injury or illness experienced by that diver since the
      diver's most recent dive and any restrictions imposed pursuant to
      section 60 by a diving doctor as a result of the diving doctor's
      examination of that diver after an injury or illness;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diver who has experienced an
    injury or illness other than decompression sickness since the
    diver's most recent dive, the diver has received approval for
    further diving from a diving doctor or a hyperbaric first-aid
    technician who consulted with a diving doctor concerning the injury
    or illness;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diver who has experienced
    decompression sickness type I, at least two days have elapsed since
    the diver successfully completed recompression therapy;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diver who has experienced
    decompression sickness type II, at least five days have elapsed
    since the diver successfully completed recompression therapy and the
    diver has received approval for further diving from a diving doctor;
    and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diver who has experienced
    decompression sickness type I in two consecutive dives, the diver
    has received approval for further diving from a diving doctor.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No diver shall make a saturation dive unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diver whose most recent dive was a
    saturation dive of 14 days' duration or less, at least 14 days have
    elapsed since the diver completed decompression; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diver whose most recent dive was a
    saturation dive of more than 14 days' duration, at least 28 days
    have elapsed since the diver completed decompression.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a diver who is employed in a diving operation
  believes the diver is unfit or unable to dive at any time during that
  employment, the diver shall so inform the supervisor of the diving
  operation and shall give the reason for that belief.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a diver becomes aware of any oil or other
  contaminant in waters in which a diving operation is being conducted,
  the diver shall forthwith inform the supervisor of the diving
  operation of the contaminant.

### [Diver's Logbooks]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917491 .Subheading}

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  diver shall keep a logbook that is permanently bound, has numbered
  pages and contains the name and signature of the diver and a
  photograph that is a likeness of the diver.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A diver shall, as soon as possible after making a
  dive, enter in the diver's logbook referred to in subsection (1), for
  each dive made by the diver,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of the dive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the diving contractor, if any, who
    conducted the dive;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of the operator or the operator's
    representative responsible for the dive;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name or other designation and the location of
    the craft or installation from which, or other dive site at which,
    the dive was conducted;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the dive identification number referred to in
    paragraph 50(1)(e);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the supervisor of the dive;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the maximum depth, the bottom time and the total
    dive time of the dive;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the decompression table and the schedule in that
    decompression table that were used in the dive;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the decompression procedures followed by the diver;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the type of personal diving equipment used by the
    diver;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} any injury suffered by the diver during the dive;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the work performed by the diver;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a description of any discomfort or illness,
    including decompression sickness, suffered by the diver; and

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} any other factor relevant to the safety or health
    of the diver.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A diver shall, after completion of an entry in the
  diver's logbook in accordance with subsection (2), immediately sign
  the entry and request the supervisor of the dive to countersign the
  entry as soon as possible.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall make any alteration to an entry in a
  diver's logbook referred to in subsection (1) unless the alteration is
  initialled by the diver and by the supervisor who countersigned the
  entry.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A diver shall produce, on request, the diver's
  logbook referred to in subsection (1) for inspection by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a conservation engineer pursuant to paragraph 43(b)
    of the Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the diving doctor who examines the diver for the
    purposes of these Regulations, at the time of the examination.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A diver shall keep in the diver's logbook referred to
  in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the diver's diving certificate or equivalent
    document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any certificates or other evidence of qualification
    in addition to those referred to in paragraph (a); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any certificates or other evidence of medical
    examination received from a diving doctor.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A diver shall retain the diver's logbook referred to
  in subsection (1) for a period of not less than two years after the
  day of the last entry made in it.

## [PART VII]{.HLabel1}[Pilots]{.HTitleText1} {#h-917519 .Part}

### [ADS Dives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917520 .Subheading}

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** No person shall pilot an
ADS in a diving operation unless the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is 18 years of age or older;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been certified to be medically fit

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to dive, in accordance with paragraph 53(b), or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to pilot an ADS, by a medical doctor who has
    examined the person not more than 12 months prior to the date on
    which the diving operation is to be conducted and who has recorded
    the results of the examination on a medical examination record in
    the form set out in Schedule V or in another form acceptable to the
    Minister and on a pilot's medical certificate in that person's
    pilot's logbook referred to in section 69;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} has delivered a copy of the medical certificate
  referred to in paragraph 53(b), or the medical certificate referred to
  in subparagraph (b)(ii), to the diving contractor who conducts the
  diving operation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid pilot's certificate issued pursuant to
  section 65 or 71, or a valid document that is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} issued by a country other than Canada on the basis
    of training and experience that are equivalent to the training and
    experience described in paragraph 65(1)(a), and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} acceptable to the Minister; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} has satisfied the supervisor of the diving operation
  that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person is capable of using, and has sufficient
    experience in the use of, the type of ADS and associated equipment
    to be used in the diving operation and is familiar with the relevant
    provisions of the procedures manual referred to in paragraph 4(4)(a)
    and the contingency plan referred to in paragraph 4(4)(i) to be used
    in the diving operation, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's involvement in the diving operation
    is in no way contrary to any restriction

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} inserted in that person's pilot's certificate or
      attached to that person's document referred to in paragraph (d)
      pursuant to section 66, or

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} inserted in that person's pilot's medical
      certificate pursuant to section 60 or 67.

### [Pilot's Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917535 .Subheading}

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a pilot's certificate that is
  valid for one year to a person who has attained a standard of
  competence in the operation of an ADS that is acceptable to the
  Minister and who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has successfully completed at least 40 hours of
    technical training in the design, construction, use and maintenance
    of an ADS at a school, institution or company acceptable to the
    Minister and who has made at least 25 ADS dives under various
    conditions with a bottom time totalling at least 40 hours;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a pilot's certificate that was issued pursuant
    to this subsection but is no longer valid because it was not renewed
    pursuant to subsection (2) and who has made at least six ADS dives
    with a bottom time totalling at least 20 hours to an average depth
    of at least 20 m during the 12 months preceding the application;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 1990, has satisfied the Minister
    that the person's training and experience are equivalent to the
    training and experience described in paragraph (a); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} holds a valid document referred to in paragraph
    64(d).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by the holder of a
  pilot's certificate issued pursuant to subsection (1), renew the
  certificate for a period of one year where the holder of that
  certificate has made at least four ADS dives with a dive time
  totalling at least 16 hours during the 12 months preceding the
  application.

### [Restrictions Respecting Pilot's Certificates and Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917543 .Subheading}

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may insert in a pilot's certificate issued pursuant to
  section 65 or 71, or attach to a document referred to in paragraph
  64(d), restrictions on the piloting of an ADS by the holder of the
  certificate or document where the Minister considers those
  restrictions necessary for safety reasons.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister inserts a restriction in a
  certificate or attaches a restriction to a document pursuant to
  subsection (1), the Minister shall give the holder of the certificate
  or document an opportunity to show cause why that restriction should
  not be so inserted or attached.

### [Restrictions Respecting Medical Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917547 .Subheading}

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  medical doctor who examines a pilot for the purposes of subparagraph
  64(b)(ii) or section 71 may insert in the pilot's medical certificate
  medical restrictions on the pilot where the doctor considers those
  restrictions necessary for safety reasons.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a medical doctor inserts medical restrictions
  in a pilot's medical certificate pursuant to subsection (1) or
  certifies in a medical certificate in a pilot's logbook that the pilot
  is medically unfit to pilot an ADS, the Minister shall, on application
  by the pilot within one month after the insertion or certification by
  the doctor, review the certificate and the pilot's medical examination
  record related to the certificate with one or more medical doctors.

### [Invalidation of Pilot's Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917551 .Subheading}

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may invalidate a pilot's certificate issued pursuant to
  section 65 or 71 where, in the opinion of the Minister, the holder of
  the certificate has become incompetent or incapacitated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister proposes to invalidate a pilot's
  certificate pursuant to subsection (1), the Minister shall give the
  holder of the certificate at least 30 days' notice in writing setting
  out the reasons for the proposed invalidation and shall give the
  holder an opportunity to show cause why the certificate should not be
  invalidated.

### [Pilot's Logbooks]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917555 .Subheading}

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  pilot shall keep a logbook that is permanently bound, has numbered
  pages and contains the name and signature of the pilot and a
  photograph that is a likeness of the pilot.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A pilot shall, as soon as possible after making a
  dive, enter in the pilot's logbook referred to in subsection (1), for
  each dive made by the pilot,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of the dive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the diving contractor, if any, who
    conducted the dive;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of the operator or the operator's
    representative responsible for the dive;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name or other designation and the location of
    the craft or installation from which, or other dive site at which,
    the dive was conducted;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the dive identification number referred to in
    paragraph 50(1)(e);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the ADS supervisor who supervised the
    dive;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the maximum depth, the bottom time and the total
    dive time of the dive;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the work performed by the pilot;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a description of any discomfort, injury or illness
    suffered by the pilot; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} any other factor relevant to the safety or health
    of the pilot.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A pilot shall, after completion of an entry in the
  pilot's logbook in accordance with subsection (2), immediately sign
  the entry and request the ADS supervisor who supervised the dive to
  countersign the entry as soon as possible.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall make any alteration to an entry in a
  pilot's logbook referred to in subsection (1) unless the alteration is
  initialled by the pilot and by the ADS supervisor who countersigned
  the entry.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} A pilot shall produce, on request, the pilot's
  logbook referred to in subsection (1) for inspection by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a conservation engineer pursuant to paragraph 43(b)
    of the Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the diving doctor or medical doctor who examines
    the pilot for the purposes of these Regulations, at the time of the
    examination.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A pilot shall keep in the pilot's logbook referred to
  in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the pilot's certificate or equivalent document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any certificates or other evidence of qualification
    in addition to those referred to in paragraph (a); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any certificates or other evidence of medical
    examination received from a diving doctor or medical doctor.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A pilot shall retain the pilot's logbook referred to
  in subsection (1) for a period of not less than two years after the
  day of the last entry made in it.

## [PART VIII]{.HLabel1}[Additional Provisions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-917579 .Part}

### [Persons Who Have First-Aid or Medical Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917580 .Subheading}

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  specialized diving doctor who is involved in a diving operation shall
  not assume responsibility for any aspect of the operation other than
  the medical aspect.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who has first-aid or medical training and
  who is employed in a diving operation shall report, without delay, to
  the supervisor of the diving operation any medical consultation the
  person had involving a diver or pilot employed in the diving operation
  and any medical advice or treatment the person provided to the diver
  or pilot.

### [Permanent Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917584 .Subheading}

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person holds a certificate issued by the Minister pursuant to
  section 28, 30, 32, 34, 54, 56, 58 or 65, or holds a valid document
  that has been accepted by the Minister pursuant to paragraph 27(c),
  29(c), 31(c) or 33(d), for at least five years, the Minister may, on
  application, issue the person a certificate, for the same category as
  the certificate or document that was held, that is valid, subject to
  section 36, 61 or 68, for as long as the person is certified to be
  medically fit in accordance with paragraph 27(b), 53(b) or 64(b).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person satisfies the Minister that, for any
  period of five years before the coming into force of these
  Regulations, the person would have qualified for a certificate under
  these Regulations if these Regulations had been in force during that
  period, the Minister may, on application received prior to January 1,
  1991, issue the person the certificate that the person would have
  qualified for, that is valid, subject to section 36, 61 or 68, for as
  long as the person is certified medically fit in accordance with
  paragraph 27(b), 53(b) or 64(b).

### [Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-917588 .Subheading}

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** Any contravention of
section 6, 7, 9 to 27, 29, 31, 33, 37 to 53, 55, 57, 62 to 64, 69 or 70
is an offence under the Act.

::::: section
:::: {#917590 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE I]{.scheduleLabel}[(Paragraph 4(4)(a))]{.OriginatingRef}[Procedures Manual]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-917591 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** The procedures manual for a diving program shall contain the
  standard operating procedures to be followed in any diving operation
  that will be part of the diving program and shall include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the procedures for any consultations with the
    person in charge of any craft or installation from which the diving
    operation is conducted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to be followed by each person
    involved in a dive that will be part of the diving program,
    including a diver, stand-by diver, pilot, attendant and supervisor;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each depth and each type of dive, the
    procedures for

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} conducting the dive, taking into account

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} local meteorological and sea-state conditions,
        and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} hazards such as strong currents, man-made
        structures and activities, other than diving, being conducted in
        the vicinity,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the selection of the appropriate breathing
      mixture, decompression tables and treatment tables to be used in
      the dive,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the use, inspection and maintenance of the
      diving plant and equipment, including communications and
      signalling equipment, to be used in the dive,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the lowering and recovery of a diver and the
      launching and recovery of any skip, diving bell, diving
      submersible or ADS to be used in the dive,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the completion of the diving operations logbook
      referred to in paragraph 9(5)(m) and subsection 50(1), including
      sample entries, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the making of a decision to commence, continue,
      interrupt or discontinue the dive, including any conditions to be
      taken into account in the determination; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a sample of the pre-dive checklist to be followed.
::::
:::::

::::: section
:::: {#917606 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE II]{.scheduleLabel}[(Paragraph 4(4)(i))]{.OriginatingRef}[Emergency Procedures]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-917607 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- - **1** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The contingency plan for a diving program
    shall contain the emergency procedures to be followed, where
    circumstances that are likely to endanger a diver or pilot make it
    impossible and unsafe to follow the procedures contained in the
    procedures manual for the diving program, such as

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} deteriorating environmental conditions;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unexpected weather or sea-state conditions;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} inability of a craft to maintain itself at the
      location of a dive site;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} evacuation of a craft or installation;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} evacuation of divers under pressures greater than
      atmospheric pressure;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in-water emergency transfers;

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} failure of any major component of diving plant or
      equipment; and

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} fouling of equipment below the surface that
      impairs the ability of a diver or pilot to complete a dive.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The emergency procedures referred to in
    subsection (1) shall include procedures for

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} emergency signalling between divers involved in
      the diving program and between the divers and their attendants
      using umbilicals or other suitable methods;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the provision of stand-by divers;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the provision of crafts, stand-by boats and any
      other devices to be used for rescue;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the provision of first-aid treatment and
      therapeutic decompression;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the use of the evacuation, rescue and treatment
      facilities and devices referred to in section 23 to be used in the
      diving program;

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} contacting the evacuation, rescue and treatment
      facilities referred to in section 23 and the medical services
      referred to in paragraphs 24(b) and (d) that will be used in the
      diving program;

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the operation of the emergency power supply;

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the evacuation of a craft or installation used in
      the diving program;

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the evacuation of divers under pressures greater
      than atmospheric pressure; and

    - [(j)]{.lawlabel} in-water emergency transfers.
::::
:::::

## [SCHEDULE VI]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subparagraphs 28(1)(a)(iii), 30(1)(a)(iii) and 32(1)(a)(iii))]{.OriginatingRef}[Recommendation for Category Diving Supervisor's Certificate]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-917785 .scheduleLabel}
:::

This is to certify that ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid}, born on ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid}, 19![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid} at ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid}, presently working for ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid}, as a Category ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid}![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid}, is familiar with all the
aspects of diving practice and supervision of that category ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid}, as specified under the [Canada
Oil and Gas Diving Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-600). Therefore,
I/we, the undersigned, have no hesitation in recommending this applicant
as a category ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-solid} diving
supervisor and, to the best of my/our knowledge and belief, I/we state
that I/we know the applicant sufficiently and that I am/we are not aware
of any reason why the applicant should not be granted the
above-mentioned status.

in doubt, ask the advice of the examining doctor.
